\langle\widetilde{C}(h),{\cal W}\rangle_{\rm HS}=\langle C(h),\widetilde{\cal W}\rangle_{\rm HS}
, where
\widetilde{\cal W}=\Pi_{n}{\cal W}\Pi_{n}
is unitary for every unitary
{\cal W}
.
Thus, the condition
\int\|C(h)\|_{\rm tr}dh<\infty
ensures that the spectral density
\tilde{f}
of
\{\widetilde{X}(t)\}
is well defined, and satisfies

\tilde{f}(\theta)=\frac{1}{(2\pi)^{d}}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}}e^{\mathbbm{i}h^{\top}\theta}\widetilde{C}(h)dh.

Following the approach in (
3.4
) based on the data
\widetilde{X}(t_{i})
, define

\tilde{f}_{n}(\theta)=\Pi_{n}\hat{f}_{n}(\theta)\Pi_{n}.

Consider the estimation of
\tilde{f}
by
\tilde{f}_{n}
. To keep the presentation simple we focus on the Gaussian case.
The following result follows readily from Theorem
4.3
.

Theorem 8.1
.

Let the process
\left\{X(t),t\in\mathbb{R}^{d}\right\}
be a zero-mean stationary Gaussian process taking values in
\mathbb{H}
. Suppose that Assumptions
3
,
3
, and
3
hold.
If, additionally we have

\displaystyle\Delta_{n}\cdot S_{K}\subset T_{n}-T_{n}\hskip 5.69046pt\mbox{for all $n$.}

Then, for any bounded set
\Theta
,

\displaystyle\sup_{\theta\in\Theta}\Big(\mathbb{E}\Big\|\tilde{f}_{n}(\theta)-\tilde{f}(\theta){\Big\|}_{\rm HS}^{2}{\Big)}^{1/2}=\mathscr{O}\left(\delta_{n}^{\gamma}+B_{1}(\Delta_{n})+B_{2}(\Delta_{n})+\sqrt{\frac{\Delta_{n}^{d}}{|T_{n}|}}\right),

(8.3)

as
n\to\infty
.

Note that, in (
8.3
), we bounded
\widetilde{B}_{1}(\Delta_{n}),\widetilde{B}_{2}(\Delta_{n})
, the counterparts of
B_{1}(\Delta_{n}),B_{2}(\Delta_{n})
where
C(h)
therein is replaced by
\widetilde{C}(h)
, by
B_{1}(\Delta_{n}),B_{2}(\Delta_{n})
, respectively. This is achieved using the simple fact that
\|\mathscr{T}_{1}\mathscr{T}_{2}\|_{\rm HS}\leq\|\mathscr{T}_{1}\|\|\mathscr{T}_{2}\|_{\rm HS}
where
\|\mathscr{T}_{1}\|
stands for the operator norm of
\mathscr{T}_{1}
.
In view of Theorem
8.1
, to find the rate of

\mathbb{E}\|\tilde{f}_{n}(\theta)-f(\theta)\|_{\rm HS}^{2}
, it is sufficient to consider the bias
\|\tilde{f}(\theta)-f(\theta)\|_{\rm HS}
,
which must be evaluated case by case, depending on the type
of a RKHS being considered. Below, we consider an example that leads to a specific rate.

Consider the Sobolev space
\mathbb{H}=W_{1}[0,1]
which consists of functions on the interval
[0,1]
of the form
c+\int_{0}^{1}(t\wedge u)h(u)du,c\in\mathbb{R}
and
h
integrable
(
Wahba 1990
, cf.)
. The inner-product in this space is

\langle f,g\rangle_{\mathbb{H}}:=f(0)g(0)+\int_{0}^{1}f^{\prime}(t)g^{\prime}(t)dt
, yielding the norm

\|g\|_{\mathbb{H}}^{2}=g(0)^{2}+\int_{0}^{1}\left(g^{\prime}(t)\right)^{2}dt.

In context, we can state the following result for
\mathbb{E}\|\tilde{f}_{n}(\theta)-f(\theta)\|_{\rm HS}^{2}
.

Theorem 8.2
.

Let the positive trace-class operator
f(\theta)
have the eigen decomposition:

f(\theta)=\sum_{j=1}^{\infty}\nu_{j}\phi_{j}\otimes\phi_{j},

where the eigenvalues
\nu_{j}
are summable (since
f(\theta)\in\mathbb{T}_{+}
). Assume that, for each
j
, the derivative

\phi_{j}^{\prime}
is Lipschitz continuous with
|\phi_{j}^{\prime}(s)-\phi_{j}^{\prime}(t)|\leq C_{j}|s-t|
for some finite constant
C_{j}
where
\sum_{j=1}^{\infty}C_{j}\nu_{j}^{2}<\infty
. Also, assume that the sampling design is
u_{n,i}=i/m_{n},0\leq i\leq m_{n}
. Then,

\|\tilde{f}(\theta)-f(\theta)\|_{\rm HS}={\cal O}(m_{n}^{-1/2}).

The proof of Theorem
8.2
is given in Section
S.6
.

9
Related work and discussions

In this section we highlight the approaches in
Panaretos and
Tavakoli 2013
and
Zhu and Politis 2020
focusing on the time-series setting and we explain how they relate to our approach.

9.1
Relation to flat-top kernel estimators

The flat-top kernel estimators have been advocated in the works of
Politis 2011
;
Zhu and Politis 2020
, among others.
According to Relation (15) of
Zhu and Politis 2020
the alternate estimator proposed in Section 3.1 therein takes the form

\frac{1}{2\pi}\sum_{|u|<T}\lambda(B_{T}u)\hat{r}_{u}(\tau,\sigma)e^{-\mathbbm{i}\omega u},

where

\displaystyle\hat{r}_{u}(\tau,\sigma)=\frac{1}{T}\sum_{0\leq t,t+u\leq T-1}X_{t+u}(\tau)X_{t}(\sigma)\ \ \mbox{ and }\ \ \lambda(s)=\int_{-\infty}^{\infty}\Lambda(x)e^{-\mathbbm{i}sx}dx

for some
\Lambda(x)
.
In the time-series setting with
d=1
, an asymptotically equivalent adaptation of our estimator in
(
5.2
) is given by:

\displaystyle\hat{f}_{T}(\theta)

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{2\pi}\cdot\frac{1}{T}\sum_{|u|<T}K\left(\frac{u}{\Delta_{T}}\right)\cdot e^{-\mathbbm{i}u\theta}\sum_{0\leq t,t+u\leq T-1}X_{t+u}\otimes X_{t}.

(9.1)

See Remark
3.2
.
Thus, the two estimators are essentially the same, with
\omega
corresponds to
\theta
,
B_{T}
to
1/\Delta_{T}
, and
\lambda
to
K
.

Zhu and Politis 2020
focuses on
p
-times differentiable flat-top kernels
\lambda
with
\lambda(t)=1
, for all
\|t\|\leq\epsilon
, for some
\epsilon>0
, where
p
is adapted to the tail decay of the covariance function. Such kernels reduce the bias of the kernel spectral density estimator in essentially the same way as do the kernels
K
satisfying (
5.8
) in the present paper. One can get a rough idea about that by the crude calculations in (
4.6
).

Moreover, in Section 5 of
Zhu and Politis 2020
, an effective data-dependent choice of the bandwidth parameter
B_{T}
is developed. The authors base their
selection on the functional version of correlogram/cross-correlogram. Using this quantity, an empirical rule is proposed for the choice of
B_{T}
. In practice, we recommend using
flat-top kernels and a similar methodology for the selection of
\Delta_{T}=1/B_{T}
. The thorough investigation of the data-driven, adaptive choice of
\Delta_{T}
in our setting
of irregularly sampled data, however, merits further theoretical and methodological investigation.

9.2
Periodogram-based estimators for functional time series

The seminal work of
Panaretos and
Tavakoli 2013
considers function-valued time series, taking values in
(L^{2}[0,1],\mathbb{R})
. They develop comprehensive theory
and methodology for inference of the spectral density operator extending the classic periodogram-based approach to the functional time series setting.
The proposed estimator therein is:

\displaystyle f_{\omega}^{(T)}(\tau,\sigma)=\frac{2\pi}{T}\sum_{s=1}^{T-1}W^{(T)}\left(\omega-\frac{2\pi s}{T}\right)p_{2\pi s/T}^{(T)}(\tau,\sigma),

(9.2)

where

W^{(T)}(x)=\sum_{j\in\mathbb{Z}}\frac{1}{B_{T}}W\left(\frac{x+2\pi j}{B_{T}}\right),

with
W
being a taper weight function of bounded support. Here,

p_{\omega}^{(T)}(\tau,\sigma)=\widetilde{X}_{\omega}^{(T)}(\tau)\widetilde{X}_{-\omega}^{(T)}(\sigma)

is the periodogram, where

\widetilde{X}_{\omega}^{(T)}=\frac{1}{\sqrt{2\pi T}}\sum_{t=0}^{T-1}X_{t}(\tau)e^{-\mathbbm{i}\omega t},

is the discrete Fourier transform (DFT). This is referred to as the smoothed periodogram estimator
(
Robinson 1983
, cf.)
.

The asymptotic properties of these periodogram-based estimators are studied using the following
general cumulant-based assumptions:

Condition
C(\ell,k)
.
For each
j=1,\ldots,k-1,

\sum_{t_{1},\ldots,t_{k-1}=-\infty}^{\infty}(1+|t_{j}|^{\ell})\|{\rm cum}(X_{t_{1}},\ldots,X_{t_{k-1}},X_{0})\|_{2}<\infty.

For example, by Theorem 3.6 in
Panaretos and
Tavakoli 2013
, if
C(1,2)
and
C(1,4)
hold, the mean
squared error of
f_{\omega}^{(T)}(\cdot,\cdot)
for
\omega\not=0,\pm\pi

is:

\mathbb{E}\|{\cal F}_{\omega}^{(T)}-{\cal F}_{\omega}\|_{\rm HS}^{2}=\mathscr{O}\Big(B_{T}^{2}+B_{T}^{-1}T^{-1}\Big),

where
{\cal F}_{\omega}^{(T)}
and
{\cal F}_{\omega}
are the operators with kernels
f_{\omega}^{(T)}
and
f_{\omega}
, respectively. The rate-optimal choice
of
B_{T}
is
T^{-1/3}
, which yields the bound on the rate of consistency of the estimator
{\cal O}(T^{-1/3})
.

Our results provide more detailed estimates on the rates under simple structural assumptions on the covariances.
Indeed, observe that the condition
C(1,2)
corresponds to our condition
{\cal P}_{D}(\beta,L)
with
\beta=1
in (
5.7
).
Our Theorem
5.2
(see Relation (
5.10
) with
d=1
) yields the rate
of consistency bound of
{\cal O}(T^{-\beta/(2\beta+1)})
, which for
\beta=1
matches the rate-optimal bound in
Panaretos and
Tavakoli 2013
.
Our condition (
5.7
), however, allows for a wider range of covariance structures than Condition
C(1,2)
, where we allow
for
\beta>0
to be less than
1
. As discussed in Section
6
, the rate
{\cal O}(T^{-\beta/(2\beta+1)})
is minimax optimal in the class
{\cal P}_{D}(\beta,L)
.

As observed in Section 3 of
Zhu and Politis 2020
, one can relate the time-domain and frequency-domain (periodogram-based) estimators. Indeed,
one can argue that our estimator in (
9.1
) corresponds asymptotically to the periodogram-based estimator in (
9.2
) with taper

W(x)=\frac{1}{2\pi}\int_{-\infty}^{\infty}e^{\mathbbm{i}tx}K(t)dt,

where
\Delta_{T}\sim 1/B_{T}
. In this case, we have
2\pi\|W\|_{2}^{2}=\|K\|_{2}^{2}
and the asymptotic covariances of
the estimators in (
9.1
) and (
9.2
) are identical
(compare, e.g., Theorem 3.7 of
Panaretos and
Tavakoli 2013
, and our Corollary
7.2
)
. Theorem 3.7 in
Panaretos and
Tavakoli 2013
establishes the asymptotic normality of the periodogram-based estimators under conditions
C(1,2)
and
C(1,4)
, as well as
C(0,k)
, for all
k\geq 2
. In Theorem
7.1
we adopt the stronger assumption that the underlying process is Gaussian. We establish, however, the asymptotic normality of our estimators under milder tail-decay conditions on the operator
covariance and pseudo-covariance functions.

Supplement

The Supplement contains the detailed proofs and some remarks.
In order to differentiate sections and results in the supplement from those in the main paper, we add a character “S" in front of sections,
lemmas, etc., in the Supplement.

For convenience, we will use the notation
a_{n}\leq_{c}b_{n}
to mean that there is a finite positive constant
B
for which
a_{n}\leq Bb_{n}
for all
n
.

S.1
Proofs for Section
4

We begin by recalling some key notation. The spectral density of the
\mathbb{H}
-valued second order
stationary process
X=\{X(t),\ t\in\mathbb{R}^{d}\}
is:

\displaystyle f(\theta):=\frac{1}{(2\pi)^{d}}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}}e^{\mathbbm{i}h^{\top}\theta}C(h)dh,\ \ \theta\in\mathbb{R}^{d},

(S.1.1)

where the last integral is understood in the sense of Bochner, and where
C(t)=\mathbb{E}[X(t)\otimes X(0)]
is the operator auto-covariance
function of
X
.

The estimator of the spectral density is defined as:

\displaystyle\begin{split}\hat{f}_{n}(\theta)&=\frac{1}{(2\pi)^{d}}\sum_{t\in\mathbb{T}_{n}}\sum_{s\in\mathbb{T}_{n}}e^{\mathbbm{i}(t-s)^{\top}\theta}\frac{X(t)\otimes X(s)}{|T_{n}\cap(T_{n}-(t-s))|}\\
&\hskip 56.9055pt\cdot K\left(\frac{t-s}{\Delta_{n}}\right)\cdot|V(t)|\cdot|V(s)|.\end{split}

(S.1.2)

Introduce also the auxiliary, idealized estimator based on the continuously sampled path
\{X(t),\ t\in T_{n}\}
:

\displaystyle g_{n}(\theta)=\frac{1}{(2\pi)^{d}}\int_{t\in T_{n}}\int_{s\in T_{n}}e^{\mathbbm{i}(t-s)^{\top}\theta}\frac{X(t)\otimes X(s)}{|T_{n}\cap(T_{n}-(t-s))|}K\left(\frac{t-s}{\Delta_{n}}\right)dtds.

(S.1.3)

S.1.1
Proof of Theorem
4.1

We begin by recalling the statement.

Theorem S.1.1
(Theorem
4.1
).

Let Assumptions
3
,
3
, and
3
hold and suppose
\delta_{n}\vee|T_{n}|^{-1}\to 0
.
Choose
\Delta_{n}\to\infty
such that

\displaystyle\Delta_{n}\cdot S_{K}\subset T_{n}-T_{n}\hskip 5.69046pt\mbox{for all $n$, }

where
A-B:=\{a-b:\ a\in A,b\in B\}
for sets
A,B\subset\mathbb{R}^{d}
.
Then, for any bounded set
\Theta\subset\mathbb{R}^{d}
, we have

\displaystyle\sup_{\theta\in\Theta}\left\|\mathbb{E}\hat{f}_{n}(\theta)-f(\theta)\right\|_{\rm HS}=\mathscr{O}\left(\delta_{n}^{\gamma}+B_{1}(\Delta_{n})+B_{2}(\Delta_{n})\right),

(S.1.4)

where

\displaystyle B_{1}(\Delta_{n})

\displaystyle:=\left\|\int_{h\in\Delta_{n}\cdot S_{K}}e^{\mathbbm{i}h^{\top}\theta}C(h)\left(1-K\left(\frac{h}{\Delta_{n}}\right)\right)dh\right\|_{\rm HS},

\displaystyle B_{2}(\Delta_{n})

\displaystyle:=\left\|\int_{h\not\in\Delta_{n}\cdot S_{K}}e^{\mathbbm{i}h^{\top}\theta}C(h)dh\right\|_{\rm HS}.

By the triangle inequality,

\displaystyle\left\|\mathbb{E}\hat{f}_{n}(\theta)-f(\theta)\right\|_{\rm HS}\leq\left\|\mathbb{E}\hat{f}_{n}(\theta)-\mathbb{E}g_{n}(\theta)\right\|_{\rm HS}+\left\|\mathbb{E}g_{n}(\theta)-f(\theta)\right\|_{\rm HS}.

It is immediate from (
S.1.1
) for
f
and the inclusion

\Delta_{n}\cdot S_{K}\subset T_{n}-T_{n}
, that

\|\mathbb{E}g_{n}(\theta)-f(\theta)\|_{\rm HS}\leq B_{1}(\Delta_{n})+B_{2}(\Delta_{n}).

To complete the proof one needs to show that

\|\mathbb{E}\hat{f}_{n}(\theta)-\mathbb{E}g_{n}(\theta)\|_{\rm HS}={\cal O}(\delta_{n}^{\gamma}).

(S.1.5)

To evaluate
\|\mathbb{E}\hat{f}_{n}(\theta)-\mathbb{E}g_{n}(\theta)\|_{\rm HS}
, first denote the integrand in
(
S.1.3
) by

\displaystyle h_{n}(t,s;\theta):=e^{\mathbbm{i}(t-s)^{\top}\theta}\frac{X(t)\otimes X(s)}{|T_{n}\cap(T_{n}-(t-s))|}K\left(\frac{t-s}{\Delta_{n}}\right).

In view of (
S.1.2
) and since
|V(w)|\cdot|V(v)|=\int_{t\in V(w)}\int_{s\in V(v)}\mathbbm{1}_{\left(t\in V(w),s\in V(v)\right)}dtds
,
this allows us to write:

\displaystyle\begin{split}&g_{n}(\theta)-\hat{f}_{n}(\theta)\\
&=\frac{1}{(2\pi)^{d}}\sum_{w\in\mathbb{T}_{n}}\sum_{v\in\mathbb{T}_{n}}\int_{t\in V(w)}\int_{s\in V(v)}\left(h_{n}(t,s;\theta)-h_{n}(w,v;\theta)\right)dtds.\end{split}

This implies that

\displaystyle\begin{split}&\left\|\mathbb{E}g_{n}(\theta)-\mathbb{E}\hat{f}_{n}(\theta)\right\|_{\rm HS}\\
&\leq\frac{1}{(2\pi)^{d}}\sum_{w\in\mathbb{T}_{n}}\sum_{v\in\mathbb{T}_{n}}\mathop{\int}_{t\in V(w)}\mathop{\int}_{s\in V(v)}\|\mathbb{E}h_{n}(t,s;\theta)-\mathbb{E}h_{n}(w,v;\theta)\|_{\rm HS}dtds.\end{split}

(S.1.6)

In the rest of the proof we will make use of the smoothness of
K
and
C
, and routine but technical analysis to
show that the last sum is of order
{\cal O}(\delta_{n}^{\gamma})
. This will
yield (
S.1.5
) and complete the proof of (
S.1.4
).

Recall that
S_{K}
denotes the bounded support of the kernel function
K
. This means that

K\left(\frac{t-s}{\Delta_{n}}\right)=0,\ \textrm{whenever}\ t-s\not\in\Delta_{n}\cdot S_{K}.

In each integral in the sums of (
S.1.6
) we have that
t\in V(w)
and
s\in V(v).
Thus,

t-s=t-w+w-v+v-s\in w-v+B(0,2\delta_{n}),

where we used that
\max\{\|t-w\|,\|s-v\|\}\leq\delta_{n}
, by the definition of
\delta_{n}
(
3.2
) and
B(0,r)=\{x\in\mathbb{R}^{d}\,:\,\|x\|_{2}<r\}
.

By (
S.1.6
), we have

\displaystyle\|\mathbb{E}g_{n}(\theta)-\mathbb{E}\hat{f}_{n}(\theta)\|_{\rm HS}

\displaystyle\leq\frac{1}{(2\pi)^{d}}\sum_{w\in\mathbb{T}_{n}}\sum_{v\in\mathbb{T}_{n}}\iint_{t,s\in T_{n}}\|C(t-s)-C(w-v)\|_{\rm HS}|L_{n}(w-v)|dtds

\displaystyle+\frac{1}{(2\pi)^{d}}\sum_{w\in\mathbb{T}_{n}}\sum_{v\in\mathbb{T}_{n}}\iint_{t,s\in T_{n}}\|C(t-s)\|_{\rm HS}|L_{n}(t-s)-L_{n}(w-v)|dsdt

\displaystyle=:I_{n}+J_{n},

(S.1.7)

where

L_{n}(x):=\frac{e^{\mathbbm{i}x^{\top}\theta}}{|T_{n}\cap(T_{n}-x)|}K\Big(\frac{x}{\Delta_{n}}\Big),\ \ x\in\mathbb{R}^{d}.

Observe that since
K(x/\Delta_{n})=0
for
x\not\in\Delta_{n}\cdot S_{K}
,
and
\Delta_{n}^{d}/|T_{n}|\to 0
, Lemma
S.1.4
implies that

|T_{n}\cap(T_{n}-x)|\sim|T_{n}|
uniformly in
x\in\Delta_{n}\cdot S_{K}
. This
and the boundedness of the kernel
K
imply

\sup_{x\in\mathbb{R}^{d}}|L_{n}(x)|={\cal O}\Big(\frac{1}{|T_{n}|}\Big).

(S.1.8)

Recall that by (
3.1
) in Assumption
3
, we have

\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}}\sup_{y\in B(x,\delta)}\|C(y)-C(x)\|_{\rm tr}dx\leq{\left|\kern-1.07639pt\left|\kern-1.07639pt\left|C\right|\kern-1.07639pt\right|\kern-1.07639pt\right|}_{\gamma}\cdot\delta^{\gamma},\ \ \delta\in(0,1)

(S.1.9)

Thus, for the term
I_{n}
in (
S.1.1
), using Relations (
S.1.8
) and (
S.1.9
), and the change
of variables
x:=t-s
,
we obtain

\displaystyle I_{n}

\displaystyle\leq\frac{1}{(2\pi)^{d}}\int_{t\in T_{n}}\int_{s\in T_{n}}\sup_{\begin{subarray}{c}\|t^{\prime}-t\|\leq\delta_{n}\\
\|s^{\prime}-s\|\leq\delta_{n}\end{subarray}}\|C(t-s)-C(t^{\prime}-s^{\prime})\|_{\rm HS}\cdot\sup_{\tau\in\mathbb{R}^{d}}|L_{n}(\tau)|dtds

\displaystyle\leq_{c}\frac{1}{|T_{n}|}\int_{s\in T_{n}}\Big(\int_{x\in s+T_{n}}\sup_{\ \ y\,:\,\|x-y\|\leq 2\delta_{n}}\|C(x)-C(y)\|_{\rm HS}dx\Big)ds

\displaystyle\leq_{c}\frac{1}{|T_{n}|}\int_{s\in T_{n}}{\left|\kern-1.07639pt\left|\kern-1.07639pt\left|C\right|\kern-1.07639pt\right|\kern-1.07639pt\right|}_{\gamma}\cdot\delta_{n}^{\gamma}ds={\cal O}(\delta_{n}^{\gamma}).

Next, focus on the term
J_{n}
in (
S.1.1
). We will show below that

\sup_{\|x-y\|\leq 2\delta_{n}}|L_{n}(x)-L_{n}(y)|={\cal O}\Big(\frac{\delta_{n}}{|T_{n}|}\Big).

(S.1.10)

Thus, recalling that
\|t-s-(w-v)\|\leq 2\delta_{n}
, whenever
t\in V(w)
and
s\in V(v)
, Relation (
S.1.10
) for
the term
J_{n}
in (
S.1.1
) implies

J_{n}\leq_{c}\frac{\delta_{n}}{|T_{n}|}\int_{t\in T_{n}}\int_{s\in T_{n}}\|C(t-s)\|_{\rm HS}dtds={\cal O}(\delta_{n}),

where the last relation follows from a change of variables
x:=t-s
and Assumption
3
(a).

To complete the proof, it remains to establish (
S.1.10
). By adding and subtracting terms, we obtain

\displaystyle|L_{n}(x)-L_{n}(y)|

\displaystyle\leq|K(x/\Delta_{n})|\Big|\frac{1}{|T_{n}\cap(T_{n}-x)|}-\frac{1}{|T_{n}\cap(T_{n}-y)|}\Big|

(S.1.11)

\displaystyle+\frac{|K(x/\Delta_{n})-K(y/\Delta_{n})|}{|T_{n}\cap(T_{n}-y)|}+\frac{|K(y/\Delta_{n})|}{|T_{n}\cap(T_{n}-y)|}\Big|e^{\mathbbm{i}x^{\top}\theta}-e^{\mathbbm{i}y^{\top}\theta}\Big|

(S.1.12)

\displaystyle=:A+B+C.

(S.1.13)

Note that
K(y/\Delta_{n})
and
K(x/\Delta_{n})
vanish whenever
x
and
y
are outside
\Delta_{n}\cdot S_{K}
. Therefore, since

\delta_{n}\to 0
and
\Delta_{n}\to\infty
, the right-hand side of (
S.1.11
) vanishes
for all
\|x-y\|\leq 2\delta_{n}
such that
\|y\|\geq{\rm const}\cdot\Delta_{n}
. Therefore,
the supremum in (
S.1.10
) does not change if it is taken over the set

{\cal I}_{n}:=\{(x,y)\,:\,\|x-y\|\leq 2\delta_{n},\ \|y\|\leq{\rm const}\cdot\Delta_{n}\}.

Thus, we restrict our attention to
(x,y)\in{\cal I}_{n}
.
By Lemma
S.1.4
, we have

|T_{n}\cap(T_{n}-y)|\sim|T_{n}|
, uniformly in
(x,y)\in{\cal I}_{n}
. This fact and the Lipschitz property of the complex exponentials and the kernel
K
(by (c)
of Assumption
3
), immediately imply that

B\leq_{c}\frac{\delta_{n}/\Delta_{n}}{|T_{n}|}\ \ \mbox{ and }\ \ C\leq_{c}\frac{\delta_{n}}{|T_{n}|},

uniformly in
(x,y)\in{\cal I}_{n}
.

Now, for term
A
, exploiting the boundedness of the kernel and the fact that
\|x-y\|\leq 2\delta_{n}
, we obtain

\displaystyle A

\displaystyle\leq\|K\|_{\infty}\frac{\Big||T_{n}\cap(T_{n}-x)|-|T_{n}\cap(T_{n}-y)|\Big|}{|T_{n}\cap(T_{n}-x)|\cdot|T_{n}\cap(T_{n}-y)|}

\displaystyle\leq\|K\|_{\infty}\frac{|T_{n}+B(0,2\delta_{n})|-|T_{n}|}{|T_{n}\cap(T_{n}-x)|\cdot|T_{n}\cap(T_{n}-y)|}

\displaystyle\leq_{c}\frac{|T_{n}+B(0,2\delta_{n})|-|T_{n}|}{|T_{n}|^{2}}=\mathscr{O}\left(\frac{\delta_{n}}{|T_{n}|^{1+1/d}}\right),

where the last inequality follows from Lemma
S.1.4
and Assumption
3
. Note that the last
bound is uniform in
(x,y)\in{\cal I}_{n}
. Combining the above bounds on the terms
A,B,
and
C
, we obtain
(
S.1.10
). This completes the proof.
∎

S.1.2
Proof of Theorem
4.2

For easy referencing, we begin by recalling the statement of Theorem
4.2
.

Theorem S.1.2
(Theorem
4.2
).

Let
X=\left\{X(t),t\in\mathbb{R}^{d}\right\}
be a zero-mean, strictly stationary real
\mathbb{H}
-valued process.
Suppose that Assumptions
3
,
3
,
3
, and
4.2
hold. Also, assume that
\Delta_{n}
satisfies

\displaystyle\Delta_{n}\cdot S_{K}\subset T_{n}-T_{n}\hskip 5.69046pt\quad\mbox{ for all $n$, and}\hskip 5.69046pt\quad\delta_{n}+\Delta_{n}^{d}/|T_{n}|\to 0\mbox{ as $n\to\infty$}.

Then

\sup_{\theta\in\Theta}\mathbb{E}\|\hat{f}_{n}(\theta)-\mathbb{E}\hat{f}_{n}(\theta)\|_{\rm HS}^{2}=\mathscr{O}\left(\frac{\Delta_{n}^{d}}{|T_{n}|}\right),\ \text{as}\ n\to\infty.

In what follows we will use
\Delta
and
T
instead of
\Delta_{n}
and
T_{n}
respectively.
Recall (
4.12
) and (
4.13
) in the main paper. Namely, we have

\displaystyle\begin{split}&\mathbb{E}\|\hat{f}_{n}(\theta)-\mathbb{E}\hat{f}_{n}(\theta)\|_{\rm HS}^{2}\\
&=\frac{1}{(2\pi)^{2d}}\sum_{t\in\mathbb{T}_{n}}\sum_{s\in\mathbb{T}_{n}}\mathop{\sum\sum}_{\begin{subarray}{c}h\in[\Delta\cdot S_{K}]\cap(\mathbb{T}_{n}-t)\\
h^{\prime}\in[\Delta\cdot S_{K}]\cap(\mathbb{T}_{n}-s)\end{subarray}}e^{\mathbbm{i}(h-h^{\prime})^{\top}\theta}K\left(\frac{h}{\Delta}\right)K\left(\frac{h^{\prime}}{\Delta}\right)\\
&\qquad\cdot|V(t+h)|\cdot|V(t)|\cdot|V(s+h^{\prime})|\cdot|V(s)|\\
&\qquad\cdot\frac{{\rm Cov}\left(X(t+h)\otimes X(t),X(s+h^{\prime})\otimes X(s)\right)}{|T\cap(T-h)||T\cap(T-h^{\prime})|}\end{split}

(S.1.14)

where

\displaystyle\begin{split}&{\rm Cov}\left(X(t+h)\otimes X(t),X(s+h^{\prime})\otimes X(s)\right)\\
&:=\mathbb{E}\left\langle X(t+h)\otimes X(t)-C(h),X(s+h^{\prime})\otimes X(s)-C(h^{\prime})\right\rangle_{\rm HS}.\end{split}

By Definition
4.1
(main paper), since
X
is real
C=\check{C}
, and

\displaystyle\begin{split}&{\rm Cov}\left(X(t+h)\otimes X(t),X(s+h^{\prime})\otimes X(s)\right)\\
&=\mathbb{E}\langle X(t+h),X(s+h^{\prime})\rangle_{\mathbb{H}}\cdot\mathbb{E}\langle X(s),X(t)\rangle_{\mathbb{H}}\\
&\hskip 14.22636pt+\langle C(t-s+h),C(s+h^{\prime}-t)\rangle_{\rm HS}\\
&\hskip 14.22636pt+{\rm cum}\left(X(t+h),X(t),X(s+h^{\prime}),X(s)\right).\end{split}

(S.1.15)

For simplicity of notation, write
{\rm cum}(s,t,u,v)={\rm cum}\left(X(s),X(t),X(u),X(v)\right)
.
We fix a real CONS
\{e_{j}\}
and use the representation in Proposition
S.5.2
(see also
(
4.9
) in the main paper). Next, we split the sum on the right-hand side of (
S.1.14
) into three terms corresponding to the decomposition (
S.1.15
). Namely, we define

\displaystyle A

\displaystyle:=\sum_{t\in\mathbb{T}_{n}}\sum_{s\in\mathbb{T}_{n}}\mathop{\sum\sum}_{\begin{subarray}{c}h\in[\Delta\cdot S_{K}]\cap(\mathbb{T}_{n}-t)\\
h^{\prime}\in[\Delta\cdot S_{K}]\cap(\mathbb{T}_{n}-s)\end{subarray}}e^{\mathbbm{i}(h-h^{\prime})^{\top}\theta}K\left(\frac{h}{\Delta}\right)K\left(\frac{h^{\prime}}{\Delta}\right)\cdot|V(t+h)|\cdot|V(t)|

\displaystyle\qquad\cdot|V(s+h^{\prime})|\cdot|V(s)|\cdot\frac{\mathbb{E}\left\langle X(t+h),X(s+h^{\prime})\right\rangle_{\mathbb{H}}\cdot\mathbb{E}\left\langle X(s),X(t)\right\rangle_{\mathbb{H}}}{|T\cap(T-h)||T\cap(T-h^{\prime})|},

\displaystyle B

\displaystyle:=\sum_{t\in\mathbb{T}_{n}}\sum_{s\in\mathbb{T}_{n}}\mathop{\sum\sum}_{\begin{subarray}{c}h\in[\Delta\cdot S_{K}]\cap(\mathbb{T}_{n}-t)\\
h^{\prime}\in[\Delta\cdot S_{K}]\cap(\mathbb{T}_{n}-s)\end{subarray}}e^{\mathbbm{i}(h-h^{\prime})^{\top}\theta}K\left(\frac{h}{\Delta}\right)K\left(\frac{h^{\prime}}{\Delta}\right)\cdot|V(t+h)|\cdot|V(t)|

\displaystyle\qquad\cdot|V(s+h^{\prime})|\cdot|V(s)|\cdot\frac{\left\langle C(t-s+h),C(s-t+h^{\prime})\right\rangle_{\rm HS}}{|T\cap(T-h)||T\cap(T-h^{\prime})|}

and

\displaystyle C

\displaystyle:=\sum_{t\in\mathbb{T}_{n}}\sum_{s\in\mathbb{T}_{n}}\mathop{\sum\sum}_{\begin{subarray}{c}h\in[\Delta\cdot S_{K}]\cap(\mathbb{T}_{n}-t)\\
h^{\prime}\in[\Delta\cdot S_{K}]\cap(\mathbb{T}_{n}-s)\end{subarray}}e^{\mathbbm{i}(h-h^{\prime})^{\top}\theta}K\left(\frac{h}{\Delta}\right)K\left(\frac{h^{\prime}}{\Delta}\right)\cdot|V(t+h)|\cdot|V(t)|

\displaystyle\qquad\cdot|V(s+h^{\prime})|\cdot|V(s)|\frac{{\rm cum}\left(X(t+h),{X(t)},{X(s+h^{\prime})},X(s)\right)}{|T\cap(T-h)||T\cap(T-h^{\prime})|}.

Thus,

(2\pi)^{d}\mathbb{E}\|\hat{f}_{n}(\theta)-\mathbb{E}\hat{f}_{n}(\theta)\|_{\rm HS}^{2}=A+B+C.

(S.1.16)

In the sequel, the bounds we shall obtain are based on the summation of the absolute values of the summands. Therefore,
in view of Lemma
S.1.4
(below) and the assumption
\Delta^{d}=o(|T|)
, the denominators in

A,B,C
can be replaced by
|T|^{-2}.

We start with the term
C
. Lemma
S.1.7
entails that

C=\mathscr{O}\left(\frac{{\cal N}(\Delta_{n}\cdot S_{K},\mathbb{T}_{n})}{|T_{n}|}\right)=\mathscr{O}\left(\frac{\Delta_{n}^{d}}{|T_{n}|}\right),

(S.1.17)

where the last relation follows from Lemma
S.1.3
.

The term
B
is bounded above by

\displaystyle|B|

\displaystyle\leq\frac{1}{|T_{n}|^{2}}\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}t,s\in\mathbb{T}_{n}\\
u\in\mathbb{T}_{n}\cap(t+\Delta\cdot S_{K})\\
v\in\mathbb{T}_{n}\cap(s+\Delta\cdot S_{K})\end{subarray}}\|C(u-s)\|_{\rm HS}\|C(v-t)\|_{\rm HS}\cdot|V(t)|\cdot|V(s)|\cdot|V(u)|\cdot|V(v)|,

where we have implemented the change of variables
u=t+h
and
v=s+h^{\prime}.
Applying Lemma
S.1.6
,
we immediately obtain that

B=\mathscr{O}\left(\frac{\mathscr{N}(\Delta_{n}\cdot S_{K},\mathbb{T}_{n})^{2}}{|T_{n}|^{2}}\right)={\cal O}\left(\frac{\Delta_{n}^{2d}}{|T_{n}|^{2}}\right),

(S.1.18)

where we applied Lemma
S.1.3
.

Finally, we steer our attention to the term
A
. Observe that

\displaystyle\left|\mathbb{E}\langle X,Y\rangle\right|=\left|\mathbb{E}\ {\rm trace}\left(X\otimes Y\right)\right|=\left|{\rm trace}\left(\mathbb{E}[X\otimes Y]\right)\right|\leq\|\mathbb{E}[X\otimes Y]\|_{\rm tr}

(S.1.19)

by (iii) of Lemma
S.7.1
. Thus,

\displaystyle|A|

\displaystyle\leq_{c}\frac{\|K\|_{\infty}^{2}}{|T|^{2}}\sum_{t\in\mathbb{T}_{n}}\sum_{s\in\mathbb{T}_{n}}\mathop{\sum\sum}_{\begin{subarray}{c}h\in[\Delta\cdot S_{K}]\cap(\mathbb{T}_{n}-t)\\
h^{\prime}\in[\Delta\cdot S_{K}]\cap(\mathbb{T}_{n}-s)\end{subarray}}\left\|\mathbb{E}[X(t+h)\otimes X(s+h^{\prime})]\right\|_{\rm tr}

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad\cdot\left\|\mathbb{E}[X(s)\otimes X(t)]\right\|_{\rm tr}\cdot|V(t+h)|\cdot|V(t)|\cdot|V(s+h^{\prime})|\cdot|V(s)|

\displaystyle=\frac{\|K\|_{\infty}^{2}\mathscr{N}(\Delta\cdot S_{K},\mathbb{T}_{n})}{|T|}\cdot A_{1}\cdot A_{2},

where

\displaystyle A_{1}=\frac{1}{|T|}\sum_{t\in\mathbb{T}_{n}}\sum_{s\in\mathbb{T}_{n}}\left\|C(s-t)\right\|_{\rm tr}\cdot|V(t)|\cdot|V(s)|

and

\displaystyle A_{2}

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{\mathscr{N}(\Delta\cdot S_{K},\mathbb{T}_{n})}\mathop{\sum\sum}_{\begin{subarray}{c}h\in[\Delta\cdot S_{K}]\cap(\mathbb{T}_{n}-t)\\
h^{\prime}\in[\Delta\cdot S_{K}]\cap(\mathbb{T}_{n}-s)\end{subarray}}\left\|C(t-s+h-h^{\prime})\right\|_{\rm tr}\cdot|V(t+h)|\cdot|V(s+h^{\prime})|.

Now

A_{2}\leq\frac{1}{\mathscr{N}(\Delta\cdot S_{K},\mathbb{T}_{n})}\max_{t,s\in\mathbb{T}_{n}}\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}u\in\mathbb{T}_{n}\cap(t+\Delta\cdot S_{K})\\
v\in\mathbb{T}_{n}\cap(s+\Delta\cdot S_{K})\end{subarray}}\|C(u-v)\|_{\rm tr}\cdot|V(u)|\cdot|V(v)|.

By Lemma
S.1.5
we obtain that
A_{2}=\mathscr{O}\left(1\right).
Moreover, a close inspection of the proof of Lemma
S.1.5
shows that
A_{1}
is also of the order
\mathscr{O}\left(1\right)
. Keeping only the dominating bounds for
A
, we have that

A=\mathscr{O}\left(\frac{\mathscr{N}(\Delta\cdot S_{K},\mathbb{T}_{n})}{|T|}\right)={\cal O}\left(\frac{\Delta_{n}^{d}}{|T_{n}|}\right),

(S.1.20)

by Lemma
S.1.3
.
In view of (
S.1.16
), gathering all the bounds in (
S.1.17
), (
S.1.18
), and (
S.1.20
), we complete the proof of the theorem.
∎

S.1.3
Lemmas used in the proofs of Theorems
4.1
and
4.2

For the next lemmas, we need to define the quantity:

\mathscr{N}(A,\mathbb{T}_{n}):=\max_{w\in\mathbb{T}_{n}}\left|\cup_{v\in\mathbb{T}_{n},w-v\in A+B(0,2\delta_{n})}V(v)\right|.

(S.1.21)

This is the maximum volume over
w
of the unions of all tessellation cells for which the representatives
v
’s
are in the
2\delta_{n}
inflated
A
neighborhood of
w
.

Lemma S.1.3
.

Let
\mathscr{N}(\Delta_{n}\cdot S_{K},\mathbb{T}_{n})
be defined as in (
S.1.21
) and suppose that (a) of Assumption
3
holds. Then

\mathscr{N}(\Delta_{n}\cdot S_{K},\mathbb{T}_{n})=\mathscr{O}\left(\Delta_{n}^{d}\right),\ \ \mbox{ as }\Delta_{n}\to\infty.

Indeed, let
Z(t):=\cup_{s\in\mathbb{T}_{n},t-s\in\Delta_{n}\cdot S_{K}+B(0,2\delta_{n})}V(s)
and
t_{0}\in\mathbb{T}_{n}.
We will show that

Z(t_{0})\subseteq B(t_{0},3\delta_{n}+\Delta_{n}\cdot M_{K}),

(S.1.22)

where
M_{k}=\sup_{h\in S_{K}}\|h\|_{2}.
Suppose
u\in Z(t_{0})
. Then, there is
s_{u}\in\mathbb{T}_{n}
such that

\|t_{0}-s_{u}\|_{2}\leq\Delta_{n}\cdot M_{K}+2\delta_{n}

with
u\in V(s_{u})
. Thus,

\|u-t_{0}\|_{2}\leq\|u-s_{u}\|_{2}+\|s_{u}-t_{0}\|_{2}\leq\delta_{n}+\Delta_{n}M_{K}+2\delta_{n}=3\delta_{n}+\Delta_{n}M_{K},

which implies (
S.1.22
). This entails that,

\displaystyle\mathscr{N}(\Delta_{n}\cdot S_{K},\mathbb{T}_{n})=\max_{t\in\mathbb{T}_{n}}|Z(t)|\leq|B(0,3\delta_{n}+\Delta_{n}M_{K})|

\displaystyle\quad\quad=\mathscr{O}\left((\Delta_{n}+\delta_{n})^{d}\right)=\mathscr{O}\left(\Delta_{n}^{d}\right),

as
\Delta_{n}\to\infty
, where the last relation follows from (a) of Assumption
3
.
∎

Lemma S.1.4
.

Under Assumption
3
, for
\|h\|_{2}\leq|T_{n}|^{1/d}
we have that

\frac{|T_{n}|-|T_{n}\cap(T_{n}-h)|}{|T_{n}|}=\mathscr{O}\left(\frac{\|h\|_{2}}{|T_{n}|^{1/d}}\right),\ \textrm{as}\ n\to\infty.

Consequently, if
\sup_{h\in A_{n}}\|h\|^{d}=o\left(|T_{n}|\right),
we have that

\sup_{h\in A_{n}}\frac{|T_{n}\cap(T_{n}-h)|}{|T_{n}|}=\mathscr{O}(1).

We will make critical use of the Steiner formula from convex analysis
(
Gruber 2007
, see, e.g.)
. We have that

\frac{|T_{n}|-|T_{n}\cap(T_{n}-h)|}{|T_{n}|}\leq\frac{|T_{n}+B(0,\|h\|_{2})|-|T_{n}|}{|T_{n}|}.

An application of Steiner formula to the convex set
T_{n}
entails that

|T_{n}+B(0,\|h\|_{2})|=\sum_{j=0}^{d}\mu_{j}(T_{n})\|h\|_{2}^{d-j},

where
\mu_{j}(\cdot)
denote the intrinsic volumes of order
j
. Note that
\mu_{d}(T_{n})=|T_{n}|.
Thus,

\displaystyle\frac{|T_{n}+B(0,\|h\|_{2})|-|T_{n}|}{|T_{n}|}

\displaystyle=\sum_{j=0}^{d-1}\frac{\mu_{j}(T_{n})\|h\|_{2}^{d-j}}{|T_{n}|}

\displaystyle=\sum_{j=0}^{d-1}\mu_{j}\left(\frac{T_{n}}{|T_{n}|^{1/d}}\right)\cdot\left(\frac{\|h\|_{2}}{|T_{n}|^{1/d}}\right)^{d-j},

where the last equality follows from the homogeneity of the intrinsic volumes.

Assumption
3
, part (b), along with the continuity of the intrinsic volumes in the Hausdorff metric on the set of convex bodies
see, e.g., Section 1.2.2 in
Lotz et al. 2018
or Theorem 6.13(iii) in
Gruber 2007
and the fact that

\|h\|_{2}\leq|T_{n}|^{1/d}
complete the proof.
∎

The following remark shows that the order of the bounds in Lemma
S.1.4

obtained using the Steiner formula cannot be improved.

Remark S.1.1
.

For the case
d=2
and
d=3
, when
T_{n}
is a circle and a sphere respectively, we can evaluate the desired volume exactly. Indeed, for
d=2,
we have that

\displaystyle\frac{|T_{n}|-|T_{n}\cap(T_{n}-h)|}{|T_{n}|}

\displaystyle=1-\frac{2}{\pi}\arccos\left(\frac{\|h\|_{2}}{2|T_{n}|^{1/2}}\right)+\frac{1}{2|T_{n}|^{1/2}}\|h\|_{2}\sqrt{4-\left(\frac{\|h\|_{2}}{|T_{n}|^{1/2}}\right)^{2}}

\displaystyle=\mathscr{O}\left(\frac{\|h\|_{2}}{|T_{n}|^{1/2}}\right),

and for
d=3
we have that

\displaystyle\frac{|T_{n}|-|T_{n}\cap(T_{n}-h)|}{|T_{n}|}

\displaystyle=\frac{3}{4}\frac{\|h\|_{2}}{|T_{n}|^{1/3}}-\frac{1}{16}\left(\frac{\|h\|_{2}}{|T_{n}|^{1/3}}\right)^{3}=\mathscr{O}\left(\frac{\|h\|_{2}}{|T_{n}|^{1/3}}\right).

These two cases provide evidence that the application of Steiner formula is not giving us a loose upper bound, at least when
T_{n}
is an
n
-dimensional ball.

Now, when
T_{n}
is a square, and assuming that
S_{n}
is the side of the square, we have that

\displaystyle\max\frac{|T_{n}|-|T_{n}\cap(T_{n}-h)|}{|T_{n}|}=\frac{S_{n}\cdot\|h\|_{2}\cdot\sqrt{2}-\|h\|_{2}^{2}/2}{S_{n}^{2}}=\mathscr{O}\left(\frac{\|h\|_{2}}{|T_{n}|^{1/2}}\right).

Finally, when
T_{n}
is a cube of side
S_{n},
we have

\displaystyle\max\frac{|T_{n}|-|T_{n}\cap(T_{n}-h)|}{|T_{n}|}=\frac{\frac{3}{2}\cdot S_{n}^{2}\cdot\|h\|_{2}-\frac{\sqrt{2}}{2}\cdot\|h\|_{2}^{3}}{S_{n}^{3}}=\mathscr{O}\left(\frac{\|h\|_{2}}{|T_{n}|^{1/3}}\right).

This suggests that using
n
-dimensional cubes leads indeed to the same rates as for the
n
-dimensional balls.

Lemma S.1.5
.

Let
\{A_{n}\}
be a growing sequence of open sets such that
A_{n}\uparrow\mathbb{R}^{d}
,
as
n\to\infty
. Moreover, let
\mathbb{T}_{n}
be the set of representatives of a tessellation of
T_{n},
with the diameter
\delta_{n}\to 0
, as
n\to\infty
,
where
T_{n}
is as in (
3.4
). Also, let Assumptions
3
,
3
and
3
hold.
Then

\displaystyle\frac{1}{\mathscr{N}(A_{n},\mathbb{T}_{n})}\max_{t,s\in\mathbb{T}_{n}}\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}u\in\mathbb{T}_{n}\cap(t+A_{n})\\
v\in\mathbb{T}_{n}\cap(s+A_{n})\end{subarray}}\|C(u-v)\|_{\rm tr}\cdot|V(u)|\cdot|V(v)|

\displaystyle=\mathscr{O}\left(\delta_{n}^{\gamma}+1\right)={\cal O}(1),

as
n\to\infty
, where
\mathscr{N}(\cdot,\cdot)
is defined in (
S.1.21
).

Using the inequality

\Big|\max_{i=1,\cdots,m}a_{i}-\max_{j=1,\cdots,m}b_{j}\Big|\leq\max_{i=1,\cdots,m}|a_{i}-b_{i}|,

valid for all
a_{i},b_{i}\in\mathbb{R},\ i=1,\cdots,m
, we obtain

\displaystyle\Bigg|\max_{t,s\in\mathbb{T}_{n}}\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}u\in\mathbb{T}_{n}\cap(t+A_{n})\\
v\in\mathbb{T}_{n}\cap(s+A_{n})\end{subarray}}\|C(u-v)\|_{\rm tr}\cdot|V(u)|\cdot|V(v)|

\displaystyle\hskip 85.35826pt-\max_{t,s\in\mathbb{T}_{n}}\iint_{\begin{subarray}{c}h\in\cup_{u\in\mathbb{T}_{n}\cap(t+A_{n})}V(u)\\
h^{\prime}\in\cup_{v\in\mathbb{T}_{n}\cap(s+A_{n})}V(v)\end{subarray}}\|C(h-h^{\prime})\|_{\rm tr}dh^{\prime}dh\Bigg|

\displaystyle\leq\max_{t,s\in\mathbb{T}_{n}}\Bigg|\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}u\in\mathbb{T}_{n}\cap(t+A_{n})\\
v\in\mathbb{T}_{n}\cap(s+A_{n})\end{subarray}}\|C(u-v)\|_{\rm tr}\cdot|V(u)|\cdot|V(v)|

\displaystyle\hskip 85.35826pt-\iint_{\begin{subarray}{c}h\in\cup_{u\in\mathbb{T}_{n}\cap(t+A_{n})}V(u)\\
h^{\prime}\in\cup_{v\in\mathbb{T}_{n}\cap(s+A_{n})}V(v)\end{subarray}}\|C(h-h^{\prime})\|_{\rm tr}dh^{\prime}dh\Bigg|

\displaystyle=\max_{t,s\in\mathbb{T}_{n}}\left|\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}u\in\mathbb{T}_{n}\cap(t+A_{n})\\
v\in\mathbb{T}_{n}\cap(s+A_{n})\end{subarray}}\iint_{\begin{subarray}{c}h\in V(u)\\
h^{\prime}\in V(v)\end{subarray}}\|C(u-v)\|_{\rm tr}-\|C(h-h^{\prime})\|_{\rm tr}dh^{\prime}dh\right|

\displaystyle\leq\max_{s\in\mathbb{T}_{n}}\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}v\in\mathbb{T}_{n}\cap(s+A_{n})\end{subarray}}\int_{h^{\prime}\in V(v)}\int_{x\in\mathbb{R}^{d}}\Bigg(\sup_{y\,:\,\|x-y\|\leq 2\delta_{n}}\Big|\|C(y)-C(x)\|_{\rm tr}\Big|dx\Bigg)dh^{\prime}

\displaystyle\leq{\left|\kern-1.07639pt\left|\kern-1.07639pt\left|C\right|\kern-1.07639pt\right|\kern-1.07639pt\right|}_{\gamma}(2\delta_{n})^{\gamma}\cdot\max_{s\in\mathbb{T}_{n}}\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}v\in\mathbb{T}_{n}\cap(s+A_{n})\end{subarray}}\int_{\begin{subarray}{c}h^{\prime}\in V(v)\end{subarray}}dh^{\prime}

\displaystyle\leq{\left|\kern-1.07639pt\left|\kern-1.07639pt\left|C\right|\kern-1.07639pt\right|\kern-1.07639pt\right|}_{\gamma}(2\delta_{n})^{\gamma}\mathscr{N}(A_{n},\mathbb{T}_{n}),

where we made the change of variables
x:=h-h^{\prime}
and enlarged the domain of integration over
x\in\mathbb{R}^{d}
.
The last two inequalities follow from (
3.1
) and definition of
{\cal N}(\cdot,\cdot)
in (
S.1.21
).

To complete the proof, we show that

\frac{1}{\mathscr{N}(A_{n},\mathbb{T}_{n})}\max_{t,s\in\mathbb{T}_{n}}\iint_{\begin{subarray}{c}h\in\cup_{u\in\mathbb{T}_{n}\cap(t+A_{n})}V(u)\\
h^{\prime}\in\cup_{v\in\mathbb{T}_{n}\cap(s+A_{n})}V(v)\end{subarray}}\|C(h-h^{\prime})\|_{\rm tr}dh^{\prime}dh=\mathscr{O}\left(1\right).

With the change of variables
x=h-h^{\prime},
we have that the aforementioned term is equal to

\displaystyle\frac{1}{\mathscr{N}(A_{n},\mathbb{T}_{n})}\max_{t,s\in\mathbb{T}_{n}}\iint_{\begin{subarray}{c}x\in[\cup_{u\in\mathbb{T}_{n}\cap(t+A_{n})}V(u)-\cup_{v\in\mathbb{T}_{n}\cap(s+A_{n})}V(v)]\\
h^{\prime}\in[\cup_{v\in\mathbb{T}_{n}\cap(s+A_{n})}V(v)]\cap[\cup_{u\in\mathbb{T}_{n}\cap(t+A_{n})}V(u)-x]\end{subarray}}\|C(x)\|_{\rm tr}dh^{\prime}dx

\displaystyle\leq\max_{t,s\in\mathbb{T}_{n}}\mathop{\int}_{\begin{subarray}{c}x\in[\cup_{u\in\mathbb{T}_{n}\cap(t+A_{n})}V(u)\\
\ \ \ -\cup_{v\in\mathbb{T}_{n}\cap(s+A_{n})}V(v)]\end{subarray}}\|C(x)\|_{\rm tr}\frac{\left|[\cup_{v\in\mathbb{T}_{n}\cap(s+A_{n})}V(v)]\cap[\cup_{u\in\mathbb{T}_{n}\cap(t+A_{n})}V(u)-x]\right|}{\mathscr{N}(A_{n},\mathbb{T}_{n})}dx

\displaystyle\leq\int_{w\in\mathbb{R}^{d}}\|C(x)\|_{\rm tr}dx={\cal O}(1),

by Assumption
3
(a). The proof is complete.
∎

The next lemma is similar to Lemma
S.1.5
. It is used for the term
B
in the proof of Theorem
S.1.2
.

Lemma S.1.6
.

Let all the assumptions of Lemma
S.1.5
hold. Then

\displaystyle\begin{split}&\frac{1}{|T_{n}|^{2}}\sum_{\mathclap{\begin{subarray}{c}t,s\in\mathbb{T}_{n}\\
u\in\mathbb{T}_{n}\cap(t+A_{n})\\
v\in\mathbb{T}_{n}\cap(s+A_{n})\end{subarray}}}\|C(u-s)\|_{\rm HS}\|C(t-v)\|_{\rm HS}\cdot|V(t)|\cdot|V(s)|\cdot|V(u)|\cdot|V(v)|\\
&=\mathscr{O}\left(\frac{\mathscr{N}(A_{n},\mathbb{T}_{n})^{2}}{|T_{n}|^{2}}\right),\end{split}

(S.1.23)

as
n\to\infty
, where
\mathscr{N}(\cdot,\cdot)
is defined in (
S.1.21
).

For any
w
in
T_{n}
, let
\tau_{w}
denote the point
t_{n,i}\in\mathbb{T}_{n}
that is in the same cell as
w
; if
w
is on the boundary of a cell, then let
\tau_{w}
be any of the
t_{n,i}\in\mathbb{T}_{n}
in adjacent cells. Thus,
\|w-\tau_{w}\|_{2}\leq\delta_{n}
.
It follows that

\displaystyle\hskip 17.07182pt\sum_{\mathclap{\begin{subarray}{c}t,s\in\mathbb{T}_{n}\\
u\in\mathbb{T}_{n}\cap(t+A_{n})\\
v\in\mathbb{T}_{n}\cap(s+A_{n})\end{subarray}}}\|C(u-s)\|_{\rm HS}\|C(t-v)\|_{\rm HS}\cdot|V(t)|\cdot|V(s)|\cdot|V(u)|\cdot|V(v)|

\displaystyle=\mathop{\iiiint}_{\begin{subarray}{c}w,\,x\in T_{n}\\
h\in\cup_{u\in\mathbb{T}_{n}\cap(\tau_{w}+A_{n})}V(u)\\
h^{\prime}\in\cup_{v\in\mathbb{T}_{n}\cap(\tau_{x}+A_{n})}V(v)\end{subarray}}\|C(\tau_{h}-\tau_{x})\|_{\rm HS}\|C(\tau_{w}-\tau_{h^{\prime}})\|_{\rm HS}dh^{\prime}dhdxdw

\displaystyle\leq\mathop{\iiiint}_{\begin{subarray}{c}w,\,x\in T_{n}\\
h\in w+A_{n}+B(0,2\delta_{n})\\
h^{\prime}\in x+A_{n}+B(0,2\delta_{n})\end{subarray}}\|C(\tau_{h}-\tau_{x})\|_{\rm HS}\|C(\tau_{w}-\tau_{h^{\prime}})\|_{\rm HS}dh^{\prime}dhdxdw

\displaystyle\leq\mathop{\iiiint}_{\begin{subarray}{c}w,\,x\in T_{n}\\
h\in w+A_{n}+B(0,2\delta_{n})\\
h^{\prime}\in x+A_{n}+B(0,2\delta_{n})\end{subarray}}\sup_{\begin{subarray}{c}\lambda_{i}\in B(0,2\delta_{n}),\\
i=1,2\end{subarray}}\|C(\lambda_{1}+h-x)\|_{\rm HS}\|C(\lambda_{2}+w-{h^{\prime}})\|_{\rm HS}dh^{\prime}dhdxdw

\displaystyle=\mathop{\iiiint}_{\begin{subarray}{c}w,\,x\in T_{n}\\
\tilde{h}\in A_{n}+B(0,2\delta_{n})\\
\tilde{h}^{\prime}\in A_{n}+B(0,2\delta_{n})\end{subarray}}\sup_{\begin{subarray}{c}\lambda_{i}\in B(0,2\delta_{n}),\\
i=1,2\end{subarray}}\|C(\lambda_{1}+\tilde{h}+w-x)\|_{\rm HS}\|C(\lambda_{2}+w-\tilde{h}^{\prime}-x)\|_{\rm HS}d\tilde{h}^{\prime}d\tilde{h}dxdw

\displaystyle\leq\mathop{\iint}_{\begin{subarray}{c}\tilde{h}\in A_{n}+B(0,2\delta_{n})\\
\tilde{h}^{\prime}\in A_{n}+B(0,2\delta_{n})\end{subarray}}\left(\int_{x\in\mathbb{R}^{d}}\sup_{\lambda\in B(0,2\delta_{n})}\|C(\lambda+x)\|_{\rm HS}dx\right)^{2}d\tilde{h}^{\prime}d\tilde{h}

\displaystyle=|A_{n}+B(0,2\delta_{n})|^{2}\left(\int_{x\in\mathbb{R}^{d}}\sup_{\lambda\in B(0,2\delta_{n})}\|C(\lambda+x)\|_{\rm HS}dx\right)^{2}

which in view of Assumption
3
implies (
S.1.23
) and completes the proof, since

|A_{n}+B(0,2\delta_{n})|\asymp\mathscr{N}(A_{n},\mathbb{T}_{n}),

(S.1.24)

because
\delta_{n}/|A_{n}|\to 0
(recall (
S.1.21
).
∎

Finally, we state a lemma to handle term
C
in the proof of Theorem
S.1.2
.

Lemma S.1.7
.

Let the assumptions of Lemma
S.1.5
and Assumption
4.2
hold. Moreover, assume that the process
\{X(t)\}
is strictly stationary.
Then,

\displaystyle\frac{1}{|T_{n}|^{2}}\sum_{\mathclap{\begin{subarray}{c}t,s\in\mathbb{T}_{n}\\
u\in\mathbb{T}_{n}\cap(t+A_{n})\\
v\in\mathbb{T}_{n}\cap(s+A_{n})\end{subarray}}}\quad\Big|{\rm cum}\left(X(u),{X(t)},{X(v)},X(s)\right)\Big|\cdot|V(t)|\cdot|V(s)|\cdot|V(u)|\cdot|V(v)|

is of the order
{\cal O}({\mathscr{N}(A_{n},\mathbb{T}_{n})}/{|T_{n}|}),
where
\mathscr{N}(\cdot,\cdot)
is defined in (
S.1.21
).

Proceeding as in Lemma
S.1.6
,
It follows that

\displaystyle\hskip 17.07182pt\sum_{\mathclap{\begin{subarray}{c}t,s\in\mathbb{T}_{n}\\
u\in\mathbb{T}_{n}\cap(t+A_{n})\\
v\in\mathbb{T}_{n}\cap(s+A_{n})\end{subarray}}}\quad|{\rm cum}(u,t,v,s)|\hskip 14.22636pt\prod_{\mathclap{\tau\in\{t,s,v,u\}}}|V(\tau)|

\displaystyle=\mathop{\iiiint}_{\begin{subarray}{c}w,\,x\in T_{n}\\
h\in\cup_{u\in\mathbb{T}_{n}\cap(\tau_{w}+A_{n})}V(u)\\
h^{\prime}\in\cup_{v\in\mathbb{T}_{n}\cap(\tau_{x}+A_{n})}V(v)\end{subarray}}|{\rm cum}(\tau_{h},\tau_{w},\tau_{h^{\prime}},\tau_{x})|dh^{\prime}dhdxdw

\displaystyle\leq\mathop{\iiiint}_{\begin{subarray}{c}w,\,x\in T_{n}\\
h\in\tau_{w}+A_{n}+B(0,\delta_{n})\\
h^{\prime}\in\tau_{x}+A_{n}+B(0,\delta_{n})\end{subarray}}|{\rm cum}(\tau_{h},\tau_{w},\tau_{h^{\prime}},\tau_{x})|dh^{\prime}dhdxdw

\displaystyle\leq\mathop{\iiiint}_{\begin{subarray}{c}w,\,x\in T_{n}\\
h\in w+A_{n}+B(0,2\delta_{n})\\
h^{\prime}\in x+A_{n}+B(0,2\delta_{n})\end{subarray}}|{\rm cum}(\tau_{h},\tau_{w},\tau_{h^{\prime}},\tau_{x})|dh^{\prime}dhdxdw.

Applying (b) of Assumption
4.2
, the last expression becomes

\displaystyle\mathop{\iiiint}_{\begin{subarray}{c}w,\,x\in T_{n}\\
h\in w+A_{n}+B(0,2\delta_{n})\\
h^{\prime}\in x+A_{n}+B(0,2\delta_{n})\end{subarray}}|{\rm cum}(\tau_{h}-\tau_{x},\tau_{w}-\tau_{x},\tau_{h^{\prime}}-\tau_{x},0)|dh^{\prime}dhdxdw

\displaystyle\leq\mathop{\iiiint}_{\begin{subarray}{c}w,\,x\in T_{n}\\
h\in w+A_{n}+B(0,2\delta_{n})\\
h^{\prime}\in x+A_{n}+B(0,2\delta_{n})\end{subarray}}\sup_{\begin{subarray}{c}\lambda_{i}\in B(0,2\delta_{n}),\\
i=1,2,3\end{subarray}}|{\rm cum}(\lambda_{1}+h-x,\lambda_{2}+w-x,\lambda_{3}+h^{\prime}-x,0)|dh^{\prime}dhdxdw

\displaystyle=\mathop{\iiiint}_{\begin{subarray}{c}w,\,x\in T_{n}\\
\tilde{h},\tilde{h}^{\prime}\in A_{n}+B(0,2\delta_{n})\end{subarray}}\sup_{\begin{subarray}{c}\lambda_{i}\in B(0,2\delta_{n}),\\
i=1,2,3\end{subarray}}|{\rm cum}(\lambda_{1}+\tilde{h}+w-x,\lambda_{2}+w-x,\lambda_{3}+\tilde{h}^{\prime},0)|d\tilde{h}^{\prime}d\tilde{h}dxdw

\displaystyle=\mathop{\iiint}_{\begin{subarray}{c}y\in T_{n}-T_{n}\\
\tilde{h},\tilde{h}^{\prime}\in A_{n}+B(0,2\delta_{n})\end{subarray}}|T_{n}\cap(T_{n}-y)|\sup_{\begin{subarray}{c}\lambda_{i}\in B(0,2\delta_{n}),\\
i=1,2,3\end{subarray}}|{\rm cum}(\lambda_{1}+\tilde{h}+y,\lambda_{2}+y,\lambda_{3}+\tilde{h}^{\prime},0)|d\tilde{h}^{\prime}d\tilde{h}dy

\displaystyle\leq|T_{n}|\mathop{\int}_{\tilde{h}^{\prime}\in A_{n}+B(0,2\delta_{n})}\sup_{z\in\mathbb{R}^{d}}\mathop{\iint}_{\begin{subarray}{c}y\in T_{n}-T_{n}\\
\tilde{h}\in A_{n}+B(0,2\delta_{n})\end{subarray}}\sup_{\begin{subarray}{c}\lambda_{i}\in B(0,2\delta_{n}),\\
i=1,2,3\end{subarray}}|{\rm cum}(\lambda_{1}+\tilde{h}+y,\lambda_{2}+y,\lambda_{3}+z,0)|d\tilde{h}^{\prime}d\tilde{h}dy

\displaystyle=\mathscr{O}(\mathscr{N}(A_{n},\mathbb{T}_{n})\cdot|T_{n}|),

where the last relation is justified by Assumption
4.2
(b) and (
S.1.24
).
Note that we have applied two changes of variables; first
\tilde{h}=h-\tau_{w}
and
\tilde{h}^{\prime}=h^{\prime}-x
, and second
v=\tilde{w}-x
. This completes the proof of the lemma.
∎

S.2
Proofs for Section
5

We start this section obtaining rates on the variance of
\hat{f}_{n}(\theta)
in Section
5
.
We first establish a result that is more general than what is needed for the proofs of Section
5
. We will use it to evaluate the variance of
\hat{f}_{n}(\theta)
in the time-series setting where
\delta_{n}\equiv 1
.

Proposition S.2.1
.

Let the process
\{X(t)\}_{t\in\delta_{n}\cdot\mathbb{Z}^{d}}
be strictly stationary and suppose that Assumptions
5
,
3
,
and
5
hold. Then, for the estimator
\hat{f}_{n}(\theta)
defined in (
5.2
), we have the following upper bound on the rate of the variance

\sup_{\theta\in\Theta}\mathbb{E}\|\hat{f}_{n}(\theta)-\mathbb{E}\hat{f}_{n}(\theta)\|_{\rm HS}^{2}=\mathscr{O}\left(\frac{\Delta_{n}^{d}}{|T_{n}|}\right),\quad{\rm as}\quad n\to\infty,

where
T_{n}=\delta_{n}\cdot[0,n]^{d}
and
|T_{n}|=(n\delta_{n})^{d}
.

As before, we will use that
\|{\cal A}\|_{\rm HS}\leq\|{\cal A}\|_{\rm tr}
throughout. Recall that

\mathbb{T}_{n}=\delta_{n}\cdot\{1,\cdots,n\}^{d}
is a discrete set of
n^{d}
samples, while
T_{n}=\delta_{n}\cdot[0,n]^{d}
is a hypercube of
side
n\delta_{n}
.

We start with

\displaystyle\hat{f}_{n}(\theta)-\mathbb{E}\hat{f}_{n}(\theta)

\displaystyle=\frac{\delta_{n}^{2d}}{(2\pi)^{d}}\sum_{t\in\mathbb{T}_{n}}\sum_{s\in\mathbb{T}_{n}}e^{\mathbbm{i}(t-s)^{\top}\theta}\frac{X(t)\otimes X(s)-C(t-s)}{|T_{n}\cap(T_{n}-(t-s))|}K\left(\frac{t-s}{\Delta_{n}}\right)

\displaystyle=\frac{\delta_{n}^{2d}}{(2\pi)^{d}}\sum_{t\in\mathbb{T}_{n}}\sum_{h\in\Delta_{n}S_{K}\cap\delta_{n}\cdot\mathbb{Z}^{d}}e^{\mathbbm{i}h^{\top}\theta}\frac{X(t+h)\otimes X(t)-C(h)}{|T_{n}\cap(T_{n}-h)|}K\left(\frac{h}{\Delta_{n}}\right)\cdot\mathbbm{1}(h+t\in\delta_{n}\cdot\mathbb{Z}^{d}).

This means that

\displaystyle\hat{f}_{n}(\theta)-\mathbb{E}\hat{f}_{n}(\theta)

\displaystyle=\frac{\delta_{n}^{2d}}{(2\pi)^{d}}\sum_{t\in\mathbb{T}_{n}}\sum_{h\in\Delta_{n}S_{K}\cap(\mathbb{T}_{n}-t)}e^{\mathbbm{i}h^{\top}\theta}\frac{X(t+h)\otimes X(t)-C(h)}{|T_{n}\cap(T_{n}-h)|}K\left(\frac{h}{\Delta_{n}}\right).

Then, the variance becomes

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\|\hat{f}_{n}(\theta)-\mathbb{E}\hat{f}_{n}(\theta)\|_{\rm HS}^{2}

\displaystyle=\frac{\delta_{n}^{4d}}{(2\pi)^{2d}}\sum_{t\in\mathbb{T}_{n}}\sum_{s\in\mathbb{T}_{n}}\mathop{\sum\sum}_{\begin{subarray}{c}h\in[\Delta_{n}\cdot S_{K}]\cap(\mathbb{T}_{n}-t)\\
h^{\prime}\in[\Delta_{n}\cdot S_{K}]\cap(\mathbb{T}_{n}-s)\end{subarray}}e^{\mathbbm{i}(h-h^{\prime})^{\top}\theta}K\left(\frac{h}{\Delta_{n}}\right)K\left(\frac{h^{\prime}}{\Delta_{n}}\right)

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad\cdot\frac{{\rm Cov}\left(X(t+h)\otimes X(t),X(s+h^{\prime})\otimes X(s)\right)}{|T_{n}\cap(T_{n}-h)|\cdot|T_{n}\cap(T_{n}-h^{\prime})|}.

By Proposition
S.5.2
we obtain that
{\rm Cov}\left(X(t+h)\otimes X(t),X(s+h^{\prime})\otimes X(s)\right)
is equal to

\displaystyle\sum_{i\in I}\sum_{j\in I}{\rm cum}\left(X_{i}(t+h),{X_{j}(t)},{X_{i}(s+h^{\prime})},X_{j}(s)\right)

\displaystyle\qquad+\mathbb{E}\left\langle X(t+h),X(s+h^{\prime})\right\rangle_{\mathbb{H}}\cdot\mathbb{E}\left\langle X(t),X(s)\right\rangle_{\mathbb{H}}+\left\langle C(t-s+h),C(s-t+h^{\prime})\right\rangle_{\rm HS}.

In an analogous manner to the proof of Theorem
4.2
, we define the quantities

\displaystyle A

\displaystyle:=\delta_{n}^{4d}\sum_{t\in\mathbb{T}_{n}}\sum_{s\in\mathbb{T}_{n}}\mathop{\sum\sum}_{\begin{subarray}{c}h\in[\Delta_{n}\cdot S_{K}]\cap(\mathbb{T}_{n}-t)\\
h^{\prime}\in[\Delta_{n}\cdot S_{K}]\cap(\mathbb{T}_{n}-s)\end{subarray}}e^{\mathbbm{i}(h-h^{\prime})^{\top}\theta}K\left(\frac{h}{\Delta_{n}}\right)K\left(\frac{h^{\prime}}{\Delta_{n}}\right)

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad\cdot\frac{\mathbb{E}\left\langle X(t+h),X(s+h^{\prime})\right\rangle_{\mathbb{H}}\cdot\mathbb{E}\left\langle X(s),X(t)\right\rangle_{\mathbb{H}}}{|T_{n}\cap(T_{n}-h)|\cdot|T_{n}\cap(T_{n}-h^{\prime})|},

\displaystyle B

\displaystyle:=\delta_{n}^{4d}\sum_{t\in\mathbb{T}_{n}}\sum_{s\in\mathbb{T}_{n}}\mathop{\sum\sum}_{\begin{subarray}{c}h\in[\Delta_{n}\cdot S_{K}]\cap(\mathbb{T}_{n}-t)\\
h^{\prime}\in[\Delta_{n}\cdot S_{K}]\cap(\mathbb{T}_{n}-s)\end{subarray}}e^{\mathbbm{i}(h-h^{\prime})^{\top}\theta}K\left(\frac{h}{\Delta_{n}}\right)K\left(\frac{h^{\prime}}{\Delta_{n}}\right)

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad\cdot\frac{\left\langle C(t-s+h),C(s-t+h^{\prime})\right\rangle_{\rm HS}}{|T_{n}\cap(T_{n}-h)|\cdot|T_{n}\cap(T_{n}-h^{\prime})|}

and

\displaystyle C

\displaystyle:=\delta_{n}^{4d}\sum_{t\in\mathbb{T}_{n}}\sum_{s\in\mathbb{T}_{n}}\mathop{\sum\sum}_{\begin{subarray}{c}h\in[\Delta_{n}\cdot S_{K}]\cap(\mathbb{T}_{n}-t)\\
h^{\prime}\in[\Delta_{n}\cdot S_{K}]\cap(\mathbb{T}_{n}-s)\end{subarray}}e^{\mathbbm{i}(h-h^{\prime})^{\top}\theta}K\left(\frac{h}{\Delta_{n}}\right)K\left(\frac{h^{\prime}}{\Delta_{n}}\right)

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad\cdot\sum_{i\in I}\sum_{j\in I}\frac{{\rm cum}\left(X_{i}(t+h),{X_{j}(t)},{X_{i}(s+h^{\prime})},X_{j}(s)\right)}{|T_{n}\cap(T_{n}-h)|\cdot|T_{n}\cap(T_{n}-h^{\prime})|}.

We start with term
A
. Bounding terms by their norm (using (
S.1.19
)) and changing variables, we obtain

\displaystyle|A|

\displaystyle\leq\delta_{n}^{4d}\sum_{t\in\mathbb{T}_{n}}\sum_{s\in\mathbb{T}_{n}}\mathop{\sum\sum}_{\begin{subarray}{c}h\in[\Delta_{n}\cdot S_{K}]\cap(\mathbb{T}_{n}-t)\\
h^{\prime}\in[\Delta_{n}\cdot S_{K}]\cap(\mathbb{T}_{n}-s)\end{subarray}}\frac{|\mathbb{E}\left\langle X(t+h),X(s+h^{\prime})\right\rangle_{\mathbb{H}}\cdot\mathbb{E}\left\langle X(s),X(t)\right\rangle_{\mathbb{H}}|}{|T_{n}\cap(T_{n}-h)|\cdot|T_{n}\cap(T_{n}-h^{\prime})|}

\displaystyle\leq\delta_{n}^{4d}\sum_{t\in\mathbb{T}_{n}}\sum_{s\in\mathbb{T}_{n}}\mathop{\sum\sum}_{\begin{subarray}{c}h\in[\Delta_{n}\cdot S_{K}]\cap(\mathbb{T}_{n}-t)\\
h^{\prime}\in[\Delta_{n}\cdot S_{K}]\cap(\mathbb{T}_{n}-s)\end{subarray}}\frac{\|C(h-h^{\prime}+t-s)\|_{\rm tr}\cdot\|C(s-t)\|_{\rm tr}}{|T_{n}\cap(T_{n}-h)|\cdot|T_{n}\cap(T_{n}-h^{\prime})|}

\displaystyle=\delta_{n}^{4d}\sum_{t\in\mathbb{T}_{n}}\sum_{s\in\mathbb{T}_{n}}\|C(s-t)\|_{\rm tr}\mathop{\sum\sum}_{\begin{subarray}{c}h\in[\Delta_{n}\cdot S_{K}]\cap(\mathbb{T}_{n}-t)\\
h^{\prime}\in[\Delta_{n}\cdot S_{K}]\cap(\mathbb{T}_{n}-s)\end{subarray}}\frac{\|C(h-h^{\prime}+t-s)\|_{\rm tr}}{|T_{n}\cap(T_{n}-h)|\cdot|T_{n}\cap(T_{n}-h^{\prime})|}

\displaystyle=\delta_{n}^{4d}\sum_{w\in\mathbb{T}_{n}-\mathbb{T}_{n}}\|C(w)\|_{\rm tr}\sum_{x\in\mathbb{T}_{n}\cap(\mathbb{T}_{n}-w)}\mathop{\sum\sum}_{\begin{subarray}{c}u\in[\Delta_{n}\cdot S_{K}\cap(\mathbb{T}_{n}-(w+x))]-[\Delta_{n}\cdot S_{K}\cap(\mathbb{T}_{n}-x)]\\
v\in[\Delta_{n}\cdot S_{K}\cap(\mathbb{T}_{n}-x)]\cap\{[\Delta_{n}\cdot S_{K}\cap(\mathbb{T}_{n}-(w+x))]-u\}\end{subarray}}

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad\frac{\|C(u+w)\|_{\rm tr}}{|T_{n}\cap(T_{n}-(u+v))|\cdot|T_{n}\cap(T_{n}-v)|},

where the last equality is obtained through the change of variables
w=s-t,x=s,u=h-h^{\prime},v=h^{\prime}.

By Lemma
S.1.4
, in view of Assumption
3
(ii), and inflating slightly the sums by dropping the intersections in the summations of
u,v
we obtain that

\displaystyle|A|

\displaystyle\leq_{\rm c}\frac{\delta_{n}^{2d}\times\delta_{n}^{2d}}{|T_{n}|^{2}}\sum_{w\in\mathbb{T}_{n}-\mathbb{T}_{n}}\|C(w)\|_{\rm tr}\sum_{x\in\mathbb{T}_{n}\cap(\mathbb{T}_{n}-w),}\sum_{u\in\Delta_{n}(S_{K}-S_{K})}\|C(u+w)\|_{\rm tr}

\displaystyle\hskip 170.71652pt\sum_{v\in(\Delta_{n}S_{K})\cap(\Delta_{n}S_{K}-u)\cap\delta_{n}\cdot\mathbb{Z}^{d}}1

\displaystyle\leq_{\rm c}\frac{\delta_{n}^{2d}}{|T_{n}|^{2}}\sum_{w\in\mathbb{T}_{n}-\mathbb{T}_{n}}\|C(w)\|_{\rm tr}\cdot|T_{n}\cap(T_{n}-w)|

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad\times\sum_{u\in\Delta_{n}(S_{K}-S_{K})\cap\delta_{n}\cdot{\mathbb{Z}}^{d}}\|C(u+w)\|_{\rm tr}\cdot|(\Delta_{n}S_{K})\cap(\Delta_{n}S_{K}-u)|

\displaystyle\leq_{\rm c}\frac{\Delta_{n}^{d}}{|T_{n}|}\times\delta_{n}^{d}\sum_{w\in\delta_{n}\cdot\mathbb{Z}^{d}}\|C(w)\|_{\rm tr}\times\delta_{n}^{d}\sum_{u\in\delta_{n}\cdot\mathbb{Z}^{d}}\|C(u)\|_{\rm tr}=\mathscr{O}\left(\frac{\Delta_{n}^{d}}{|T_{n}|}\right),

by Assumption
5
, where we used that

\delta_{n}^{d}\sum_{t}1_{\{t\in\mathbb{T}_{n}\}}\sim|T_{n}|

and

\delta_{n}^{d}\sum_{t}1_{\{t\in(\Delta_{n}\cdot S_{K})\cap(\Delta_{n}\cdot S_{K}-u)\cap\delta_{n}\cdot\mathbb{Z}^{d}\}}\leq 2|\Delta_{n}S_{K}|={\cal O}(\Delta_{n}^{d}).

Now, we shift to term
B
. Using the change of variables
w:=t-s
, the Cauchy-Schwartz inequality, we obtain

\displaystyle|B|

\displaystyle\leq\delta_{n}^{4d}\sum_{w\in\mathbb{T}_{n}-\mathbb{T}_{n}}\sum_{s}1_{\{\mathbb{T}_{n}\cap(\mathbb{T}_{n}-w)\}}(s)\mathop{\sum\sum}_{\begin{subarray}{c}h\in[\Delta_{n}\cdot S_{K}]\cap\delta_{n}\cdot\mathbb{Z}^{d}\\
h^{\prime}\in[\Delta_{n}\cdot S_{K}]\cap\delta_{n}\cdot\mathbb{Z}^{d}\end{subarray}}K\left(\frac{h}{\Delta_{n}}\right)K\left(\frac{h^{\prime}}{\Delta_{n}}\right)

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad\qquad\cdot\frac{\|C(h+w)\|_{\rm HS}\|C(h^{\prime}-w)\|_{\rm HS}}{|T_{n}\cap(T_{n}-h)|\cdot|T_{n}\cap(T_{n}-h^{\prime})|}

\displaystyle\leq_{c}\frac{\delta_{n}^{3d}}{|T_{n}|}\sum_{w\in\mathbb{T}_{n}-\mathbb{T}_{n}}\mathop{\sum\sum}_{\begin{subarray}{c}h\in[\Delta_{n}\cdot S_{K}]\cap\delta_{n}\cdot\mathbb{Z}^{d}\ \\
h^{\prime}\in[\Delta_{n}\cdot S_{K}]\cap\delta_{n}\cdot\mathbb{Z}^{d}\end{subarray}}\|C(h+w)\|_{\rm HS}\|C(h^{\prime}-w)\|_{\rm HS},

where we used that
\delta_{n}^{d}\sum_{s}1_{\{\mathbb{T}_{n}\cap(\mathbb{T}_{n}-w)\}}(s)={\cal O}(|T_{n}|)
, and Lemma
S.1.4

to conclude that
|T_{n}\cap(T_{n}-h)|\sim|T_{n}\cap(T_{n}-h^{\prime})|\sim|T_{n}|
, uniformly in
h,h^{\prime}\in\Delta_{n}\cdot S_{K}
. Now,
with the change of variables
u:=h+w
, and expanding the range of summation, we further
obtain

\displaystyle|B|

\displaystyle\leq_{\rm c}\frac{\delta_{n}^{3d}}{|T_{n}|}\sum_{u\in\delta_{n}\cdot\mathbb{Z}^{d}}\|C(u)\|_{\rm HS}\sum_{h^{\prime}\in\Delta_{n}S_{K}\cap\delta_{n}\cdot\mathbb{Z}^{d}\ }\sum_{h\in\Delta_{n}S_{K}\cap(u-(\mathbb{T}_{n}-\mathbb{T}_{n}))\ }\|C(h^{\prime}-u+h)\|_{\rm HS}

\displaystyle\leq\frac{1}{|T_{n}|}\Big(\sum_{u\in\delta_{n}\cdot\mathbb{Z}^{d}}\delta_{n}^{d}\|C(u)\|_{\rm HS}\Big)\Big(\delta_{n}^{d}\sum_{h^{\prime}\in\Delta_{n}S_{K}\cap\delta_{n}\cdot\mathbb{Z}^{d}\ }1\Big)\Big(\sum_{h\in\delta_{n}\cdot\mathbb{Z}^{d}}\delta_{n}^{d}\|C(h)\|_{\rm HS}\Big)

\displaystyle=\mathscr{O}\left(\frac{\Delta_{n}^{d}}{|T_{n}|}\right),

in view of Assumption
5
.

Finally, we look at term
C
. An application of Lemma
S.1.4
, again gives us that

\displaystyle|C|

\displaystyle\leq_{\rm c}\frac{\delta_{n}^{4d}}{|T_{n}|^{2}}\sum_{t\in\mathbb{T}_{n}}\sum_{s\in\mathbb{T}_{n}}\mathop{\sum\sum}_{\begin{subarray}{c}h\in[\Delta_{n}\cdot S_{K}]\cap(\mathbb{T}_{n}-t)\\
h^{\prime}\in[\Delta_{n}\cdot S_{K}]\cap(\mathbb{T}_{n}-s)\end{subarray}}\left|\sum_{i\in I}\sum_{j\in I}{\rm cum}\left(X_{i}(t+h),{X_{j}(t)},{X_{i}(s+h^{\prime})},X_{j}(s)\right)\right|

\displaystyle\leq\frac{\delta_{n}^{4d}}{|T_{n}|^{2}}\sum_{t\in\mathbb{T}_{n}}\sum_{s\in\mathbb{T}_{n}}\mathop{\sum\sum}_{\begin{subarray}{c}h\in\Delta_{n}\cdot S_{K}\cap\delta_{n}\cdot\mathbb{Z}^{d}\\
h^{\prime}\in\Delta_{n}\cdot S_{K}\cap\delta_{n}\cdot\mathbb{Z}^{d}\end{subarray}}\left|\sum_{i\in I}\sum_{j\in I}{\rm cum}\left(X_{i}(h+t-s),{X_{j}(t-s)},{X_{i}(h^{\prime})},X_{j}(0)\right)\right|

\displaystyle\leq\frac{\delta_{n}^{3d}}{|T_{n}|}\sum_{w\in\mathbb{T}_{n}-\mathbb{T}_{n}}\mathop{\sum\sum}_{\begin{subarray}{c}h\in\Delta_{n}\cdot S_{K}\cap\delta_{n}\cdot\mathbb{Z}^{d}\\
h^{\prime}\in\Delta_{n}\cdot S_{K}\cap\delta_{n}\cdot\mathbb{Z}^{d}\end{subarray}}\left|\sum_{i\in I}\sum_{j\in I}{\rm cum}\left(X_{i}(h+w),{X_{j}(w)},{X_{i}(h^{\prime})},X_{j}(0)\right)\right|

\displaystyle\leq_{\rm c}\frac{\Delta_{n}^{d}}{|T_{n}|}\sup_{h^{\prime}\in\delta_{n}\cdot\mathbb{Z}^{d}}\delta_{n}^{2d}\sum_{w\in\delta_{n}\cdot\mathbb{Z}^{d}\ \ }\sum_{h\in\delta_{n}\cdot\mathbb{Z}^{d}}\left|\sum_{i\in I}\sum_{j\in I}{\rm cum}\left(X_{i}(h+w),{X_{j}(w)},{X_{i}(h^{\prime})},X_{j}(0)\right)\right|

\displaystyle=\mathscr{O}\left(\frac{\Delta_{n}^{d}}{|T_{n}|}\right),

where we used that
\delta_{n}^{d}\sum_{h^{\prime}\in\Delta_{n}\cdot S_{K}\cap\delta_{n}\cdot\mathbb{Z}^{d}}1={\cal O}(|\Delta_{n}|^{d})
,
the fact that
\delta_{n}^{d}\sum_{s\in\mathbb{T}_{n}\cap\mathbb{T}_{n}}={\cal O}(|T_{n}|)
, and
Assumption
5
. This completes the proof.
∎

Proof of Theorem
5.2
:
As indicated, by following the proof of Proposition
S.2.1
,
we see that the variance bound
{\cal O}(\Delta_{n}^{d}/|T_{n}|)
is uniform in
f\in{\cal P}_{D}(\beta,L)
. Therefore, to prove
(
5.9
), by Relation (
5.5
) in Theorem
5.1
, it is enough to
bound terms
B_{1}(\Delta_{n})
and
B_{2}(\Delta_{n})
, uniformly in
f\in{\cal P}_{D}(\beta,L)
.
Let
M_{k}
and
m_{k}
be the radii of the smallest ball that contains
S_{K}
and the largest ball contained in
S_{K}
respectively. Starting with term
B_{2}
we have,

\displaystyle\sup_{f\in{\cal P}_{D}(\beta,L)}B_{2}(\Delta_{n})

\displaystyle\leq\sum_{\|h\|_{2}\geq\Delta_{n}m_{k}}\sup_{f\in{\cal P}_{D}(\beta,L)}\|C(h)\|_{\rm HS}\|h\|_{2}^{\beta}\|h\|_{2}^{-\beta}

\displaystyle\leq(\Delta_{n}m_{k})^{-\beta}\sum_{\|h\|_{2}\geq\Delta_{n}m_{k}}\sup_{f\in{\cal P}_{D}(\beta,L)}\|C(h)\|_{\rm HS}\|h\|_{2}^{\beta}

\displaystyle\leq(\Delta_{n}m_{k})^{-\beta}\cdot L={\cal O}(\Delta_{n}^{-\beta}),

in view of (
5.7
). Recalling that
K(0)=1,
we have that for
\lambda+1>\beta,

\displaystyle\sup_{f\in{\cal P}_{D}(\beta,L)}B_{1}(\Delta_{n})

\displaystyle\leq\sum_{0<\|h\|_{2}\leq\Delta_{n}M_{K}}\sup_{f\in{\cal P}_{D}(\beta,L)}\left\|C(h)\right\|_{\rm HS}\left[1-K\left(\frac{h}{\Delta_{n}}\right)\right]

\displaystyle\leq\tilde{c}\sum_{0<\|h\|_{2}\leq\Delta_{n}M_{K}}\sup_{f\in{\cal P}_{D}(\beta,L)}\left\|C(h)\right\|_{\rm HS}\frac{\|h\|_{2}^{\lambda+1}}{\Delta_{n}^{\lambda+1}}

\displaystyle\leq\tilde{c}\Delta_{n}^{-\beta}M_{K}^{\lambda+1-\beta}\sum_{0<\|h\|_{2}\leq\Delta_{n}M_{K}}\sup_{f\in{\cal P}_{D}(\beta,L)}\left\|C(h)\right\|_{\rm HS}\|h\|_{2}^{\beta}=\mathscr{O}(\Delta_{n}^{-\beta}),

where the second inequality follows from the multivariate Taylor Theorem since (
5.8
) holds. Indeed, under this condition and using that
K(0)=1
, we obtain

K\left(\frac{h}{\Delta_{n}}\right)=1+R_{0,\lambda}\left(\frac{h}{\Delta_{n}}\right),

where
|R_{0,\lambda}(h)|\leq\frac{L_{1}}{(\lambda+1)!}\|h\|_{2}^{\lambda+1}.

Collecting the bounds for
B_{1}(\Delta_{n})
and
B_{2}(\Delta_{n})
, we obtain that the bias is of order
{\cal O}(\Delta_{n}^{\beta})
, uniformly over the class
{\cal P}_{D}(\beta,L)
.
Now, by Theorem
5.1
, the variance is of order
{\cal O}(\Delta_{n}^{d}/|T_{n}|)
and picking

\Delta_{n}=|T_{n}|^{1/(2\beta+d)}
, we obtain the rate-optimal bound in (
5.10
).
∎

Proof of Theorem
5.3
.

In view of Theorem
S.1.1
, one only needs to bound the
terms
B_{1}(\Delta_{n})
and
B_{2}(\Delta_{n})
appropriately. Starting with term
B_{2}
, if
m_{K}
denotes the
radius of the largest ball contained in
S_{K},
we have

\displaystyle B_{2}(\Delta_{n})

\displaystyle\leq\int_{x\not\in\Delta_{n}\cdot S_{K}}\|C(x)\|_{\rm HS}dx

\displaystyle\leq(\Delta_{n}\cdot m_{K})^{-\beta}\cdot\int_{\|x\|>\Delta_{n}\cdot m_{K}}\|x\|_{2}^{\beta}\cdot\|C(x)\|_{\rm HS}dx

\displaystyle\leq(\Delta_{n}\cdot m_{K})^{-\beta}\cdot L={\cal O}(\Delta_{n}^{-\beta}).

Next, recall that

B_{1}(\Delta_{n}):=\left\|\int_{h\in\Delta_{n}\cdot S_{K}}e^{-\mathbbm{i}h^{\top}\theta}C(h)\left(1-K\left(\frac{h}{\Delta_{n}}\right)\right)dh\right\|_{\rm HS}.

Since
K(0)=1
and (
5.8
) holds, by the Taylor theorem, we have that

K\left(\frac{h}{\Delta_{n}}\right)=1+R_{0,\lambda}\left(\frac{h}{\Delta_{n}}\right),

where
|R_{0,\lambda}(h)|\leq\frac{L_{1}}{(\lambda+1)!}\|h\|_{2}^{\lambda+1}.

Thus, with
M_{K}
denoting the radius of the smallest ball centered at the origin that contains
S_{K}
,
the term
B_{1}
is bounded by

\displaystyle B_{1}(\Delta_{n})

\displaystyle\leq\frac{L_{1}}{(\lambda+1)!}\int_{h\in\Delta_{n}\cdot S_{K}}\|C(h)\|_{\rm HS}\Big(\frac{\|h\|_{2}}{\Delta_{n}}\Big)^{\lambda+1}dh

\displaystyle\leq\frac{L_{1}\cdot(M_{K}\cdot\Delta_{n})^{\lambda+1-\beta}}{(\lambda+1)!\cdot\Delta_{n}^{\lambda+1}}\int_{h\in\Delta_{n}\cdot S_{K}}\|C(h)\|_{\rm HS}\|h\|_{2}^{\beta}dh={\cal O}(\Delta_{n}^{-\beta}),

since
\lambda+1>\beta
and in view of (
5.11
).

Collecting the bounds for
B_{1}
and
B_{2}
, we get
B_{1}(\Delta_{n})+B_{2}(\Delta_{n})=\mathscr{O}\left(\Delta_{n}^{-\beta}\right).

Now, the optimal choice of
\Delta_{n}
is the one which balances the last bound with the
rate of the variance, that is,
\Delta_{n}^{d}/|T_{n}|\sim\Delta_{n}^{-2\beta}
. This is achieved with

\Delta_{n}:=|T_{n}|^{1/(2\beta+d)}\equiv(n\delta_{n})^{d/(2\beta+d)},

which upon substitution yields the rate
\delta_{n}^{\gamma}\vee\Delta_{n}^{-\beta}=\delta_{n}^{\gamma}\vee(n\delta_{n})^{-\beta d/(2\beta+d)}
in (
5.12
).
∎

S.3
Proofs for Section
6

For any member
e_{i}
of the real CONS, consider the (scalar) real-valued process

X_{e_{i}}(t):=\langle X(t),e_{i}\rangle_{\mathbb{H}}

and let
C_{e_{i}}(x)
and
f_{e_{i}}(\theta)
be its stationary covariance and spectral density, respectively. If
f\in{\cal P}_{D}(\beta,L)
then

\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}}(1+\|x\|_{2}^{\beta})|C_{e_{i}}(x)|dx\leq L\quad\mbox{and}\quad|\hat{f}_{e_{i}}(\theta_{0})-f_{e_{i}}(\theta_{0})|\leq\|\hat{f}(\theta_{0})-f(\theta_{0})\|_{\rm HS}.

These follow from the simple fact that
|\langle{\cal A}\phi,\phi\rangle_{\mathbb{H}}|\leq\|{\cal A}\|_{\rm op}
for any bounded linear operator
{\cal A}
and unitary
\phi\in\mathbb{H}
. Thus, it suffices to prove Theorems
6.1
and
6.2
for scalar, real-valued processes, which we do below.

Let
\|\cdot\|
denote the Euclidean norm in
\mathbb{R}^{d}
and
C_{g}
be the covariance that corresponds to the spectral density
g
. Fix an interior point
\theta_{0}\in(-\pi,\pi)^{d}
and
let
f_{0,n}(\theta)=L/(2\cdot(2\pi)^{d})\cdot\mathbbm{1}(\theta\in[-\pi,\pi]^{d}).
Then,

C_{f_{0,n}}(k)=\int_{\theta\in[-\pi,\pi]^{d}}e^{-\mathbbm{i}\theta^{\top}k}\frac{L}{2\cdot(2\pi)^{d}}d\theta=\mathbbm{1}(k=0)L/2,

(S.3.1)

and therefore

\sum_{k\in\mathbb{Z}^{2}}|C_{f_{0,n}}(k)|(1+\|k\|^{\beta})=C_{f_{0,n}}(0)=L/2<L.

(S.3.2)

Let for
\theta=(\theta_{i})_{i=1}^{d}\in\mathbb{R}^{d}
,

\displaystyle g(\theta)=\epsilon\cdot\prod_{i=1}^{d}\varphi(\theta_{i}),\ \ \mbox{ where }\varphi(x)=\exp\left(-\frac{1}{1-(x/\pi)^{2}}\right)\mathbbm{1}(|x|<\pi),\ \ (x\in\mathbb{R})

(S.3.3)

for some
\epsilon>0
, which is to be determined. The function
\varphi
is a type of a “bump” function that belongs to

C_{0}^{\infty}(\mathbb{R})
(the class of infinitely differentiable functions with compact support). The support of
\varphi
is
the compact interval
[-\pi,\pi]
. Hence
g\in C_{0}^{\infty}(\mathbb{R}^{d})
and its support is
[-\pi,\pi]^{d}
. Consider the function

\displaystyle g_{n}(\theta)=h_{n}^{\beta}g\left(\frac{\theta-\theta_{0}}{h_{n}}\right),

(S.3.4)

where
0<h_{n}\leq 1
and tends to
0
at a rate to be determined later. Observe that since
\theta_{0}\in(-\pi,\pi)^{d}
, the support of

g_{n}
is included in
\theta_{0}+h_{n}\cdot[-\pi,\pi]^{d}\subset(-\pi,\pi)^{d}
, for all sufficiently small
h_{n}
.

Now, consider the “alternative” spectral density models:

\displaystyle f_{1,n}(\theta)

\displaystyle=f_{0,n}(\theta)+[g_{n}(\theta)+g_{n}(-\theta)]=:\frac{L}{2}\cdot\mathbbm{1}_{(-\pi,\pi)^{d}}(\theta)+r_{n}(\theta),\ \ \theta\in[-\pi,\pi]^{d}.

We will choose the sequence
h_{n}
and the constant
\epsilon>0
such that the following three properties hold.

Properties:

(1)

f_{0,n},f_{1,n}\in{\cal P}_{D}(\beta,L)
, where the class
{\cal P}_{D}(\beta,L)
is defined in (
5.7
).

(2)

For all
n
large enough, we have

f_{1,n}(\theta_{0})-f_{0,n}(\theta_{0})=h_{n}^{\beta}[g(0)+g(2\theta_{0}/h_{n})]=g(0)(1+\mathbbm{1}(\theta_{0}=0))\cdot h_{n}^{\beta}.

(S.3.5)

(3)

{\rm KL}(\mathbb{P}_{1n},\mathbb{P}_{0n})\leq C<\infty
, where
{\rm KL}
stands for the Kullback-Leibler distance and
\mathbb{P}_{0n}
and
\mathbb{P}_{1n}
are probability distributions of the data
\{X(k),\ k\in\{1,\cdots,n\}^{d}\}

under
f_{0,n}
and
f_{1,n}
respectively.

Proof of Property (1).
We have already shown that
f_{0,n}\in{\cal P}_{D}(\beta,L)
. Recalling (
5.7
), and in view of (
S.3.1
) and (
S.3.2
),
to prove
f_{1,n}\in{\cal P}_{D}(\beta,L)
, it is enough to show that

\sum_{k\in\mathbb{Z}^{d}}|C_{g_{n}}(k)|(1+\|k\|^{\beta})<\frac{L}{4},

(S.3.6)

where
C_{g_{n}}(k)=\int_{\theta\in[-\pi,\pi]^{d}}e^{-\mathbbm{i}\theta^{\top}k}g_{n}(\theta)d\theta
.

We have that for all
k=(k_{i})_{i=1}^{d}\in\mathbb{Z}^{d}
,

\displaystyle\begin{split}C_{g_{n}}(k)&=\int_{\theta\in[-\pi,\pi]^{d}}e^{-\mathbbm{i}\theta^{\top}k}h_{n}^{\beta}g\left(\frac{\theta-\theta_{0}}{h_{n}}\right)d\theta\\
&=h_{n}^{\beta+d}\cdot e^{-\mathbbm{i}\theta_{0}^{\top}k}\int_{x\in[-\pi,\pi]^{d}}e^{-\mathbbm{i}x^{\top}kh_{n}}g(x)dx\\
&=\epsilon\cdot h_{n}^{\beta+d}\cdot e^{-\mathbbm{i}\theta_{0}^{\top}k}\prod_{i=1}^{d}\widehat{\varphi}(k_{i}h_{n}),\end{split}

(S.3.7)

where we used the change of variables
x=(\theta-\theta_{0})/h_{n}
and the fact that
\theta_{0}+h_{n}\cdot[-\pi,\pi]^{d}\subset(-\pi,\pi)^{d}
, for all
sufficiently small
h_{n}
. The last relation follows from (
S.3.3
), where
\widehat{\varphi}(x):=\int_{-\pi}^{\pi}e^{-\mathbbm{i}xu}\varphi(u)du
denotes
the Fourier transform of the bump function
\varphi
. Now using the fact that the derivatives of
\varphi
vanish at
\pm\pi
, i.e.,

\varphi^{(\ell)}(\pm\pi)=0
, for all
\ell=0,1,\ldots
, integration by parts yields

\widehat{\varphi}(x)=\frac{1}{(-ix)^{\ell}}\int_{-\pi}^{\pi}e^{-\mathbbm{i}xu}\varphi^{(\ell)}(u)du,\ \ \ell=0,1,\ldots

Indeed, for all
\ell
, the derivative
\varphi^{(\ell)}(x)
is continuous and supported on
[-\pi,\pi]
, and thus

|\widehat{\varphi}(x)|\leq c_{0}\wedge(|x|^{-\ell}c_{\ell}),\ \ \mbox{ where }\ c_{\ell}:=\int_{-\pi}^{\pi}|\varphi^{(\ell)}(u)|du.

In view of (
S.3.7
), we have

|C_{g_{n}}(k)|\leq\epsilon\cdot h_{n}^{\beta+d}\prod_{i=1}^{d}\left(c_{0}\wedge\frac{c_{\ell}}{|k_{i}h_{n}|^{\ell}}\right).

(S.3.8)

We will choose
\ell\geq 2
and
\epsilon>0
to satisfy (
S.3.6
)
for all sufficiently small
h_{n}
. Notice that
\|k\|^{\beta}\leq d^{(\beta-1)\vee 0}\sum_{i=1}^{d}|k_{i}|^{\beta}
. Hence, (
S.3.6
) follows from

d^{(\beta-1)\vee 0}\sum_{i=1}^{d}\sum_{k\in\mathbb{Z}^{d}}|C_{g_{n}}(k)|(1+|k_{i}|^{\beta})=d^{\beta\vee 1}\sum_{k\in\mathbb{Z}^{d}}|C_{g_{n}}(k)|(1+|k_{1}|^{\beta})<\frac{L}{4},

where
k=(k_{i})_{i=1}^{d}
. Indeed, this follows from the observation that, by (
S.3.7
), we have

\sum_{i=1}^{d}\sum_{k\in\mathbb{Z}^{d}}|C_{g_{n}}(k_{1},\cdots,k_{d})||k_{i}|^{\beta}=d\sum_{k\in\mathbb{Z}^{d}}|C_{g_{n}}(k_{1},\cdots,k_{d})||k_{1}|^{\beta}.

Thus, it suffices to show that

\displaystyle\begin{split}\sum_{k\in\mathbb{Z}^{d}}|C_{g_{n}}(k)|(1+|k_{1}|^{\beta})&\leq|C_{g_{n}}(0)|+2\sum_{k=(k_{i})_{i=1}^{d}\in\mathbb{Z}^{d}}|C_{g_{n}}(k)||k_{1}|^{\beta}\\
&\leq\frac{L}{4(d^{\beta\vee 1})}.\end{split}

(S.3.9)

Since
h_{n}\in(0,1)
, (
S.3.8
) readily implies that

\displaystyle|C_{g_{n}}(0)|\leq\epsilon\cdot c_{0}^{d}.

Also, applying (
S.3.8
),

\displaystyle\begin{split}&\sum_{k=(k_{i})_{i=1}^{d}\in\mathbb{Z}^{d}}|C_{g_{n}}(k)||k_{1}|^{\beta}\\
&\leq\epsilon\cdot\sum_{k\in\mathbb{Z}^{d}}\left[h_{n}^{\beta+1}|k_{1}|^{\beta}\left(c_{0}\wedge\frac{c_{\ell}}{|k_{1}h_{n}|^{\ell}}\right)\cdot h_{n}^{d-1}\prod_{i=2}^{d}\left(c_{0}\wedge\frac{c_{\ell}}{|k_{i}h_{n}|^{\ell}}\right)\right]\\
&=\epsilon\cdot\left[h_{n}\cdot\sum_{j\in\mathbb{Z}}|jh_{n}|^{\beta}\left(c_{0}\wedge\frac{c_{\ell}}{|jh_{n}|^{\ell}}\right)\right]\times\left[h_{n}\sum_{j\in\mathbb{Z}}\left(c_{0}\wedge\frac{c_{\ell}}{|jh_{n}|^{\ell}}\right)\right]^{d-1}\\
&=:\epsilon\times A_{n}\times(B_{n})^{d-1}.\end{split}

(S.3.10)

Observe that

A_{n}
and
B_{n}
are Riemann sums for the integrals

A:=\int_{x\in\mathbb{R}}|x|^{\beta}\left(c_{0}\wedge\frac{c_{\ell}}{|x|^{\ell}}\right)dx\ \ \ \mbox{ and }\ \ \ B:=\int_{x\in\mathbb{R}}\left(c_{0}\wedge\frac{c_{\ell}}{|x|^{\ell}}\right)dx,

which are clearly finite for
\ell\geq\lfloor\beta\rfloor+2
. Taking such a value of
\ell
and using
the fact that
A_{n}\to A
and
B_{n}\to B
, as
h_{n}\to 0
, we obtain that the right hand side of (
S.3.10
) is bounded above by

2\epsilon\times A\times B^{d-1}
for all sufficiently small
h_{n}
.
Therefore, we can ensure that (
S.3.9
) holds by picking
\epsilon>0
such that

0<\epsilon\cdot\Big[c_{0}^{d}+4A\times B^{d-1}\Big]\leq\frac{L}{4(d^{\beta\vee 1})}.

This shows that
f_{1,n}\in{\cal P}_{D}(\beta,L)
and completes the proof of Property (1).

Proof of Property (2).
This is immediate. Relation (
S.3.5
) holds for all sufficiently large
n
since

g(\theta_{0}/h_{n})\to g(0)\mathbbm{1}(\theta_{0}=0),
as
h_{n}\to 0
, by the fact that
g
is
supported on
[-\pi,\pi]
.

Proof of Property (3).
Let
D_{n}
and
B_{n,\xi}
be the covariance matrices of the data
X(t),t\in\{1,\ldots,n\}^{d}
, that correspond to,
respectively, the spectral densities
r_{n}(\theta)=g_{n}(\theta)+g_{n}(-\theta)
and

f_{0,n}(\theta)+\xi r_{n}(\theta),
for some
\xi\in[0,1].
By Lemma
S.3.2
,

\mathrm{KL}(\mathbb{P}_{1n},\mathbb{P}_{0n})\leq\frac{1}{2}\|D_{n}\|_{\rm F}^{2}\|B_{n,\xi}^{-1}\|_{\rm op}^{2},

(S.3.11)

where
\|\cdot\|_{\rm F}
is the Frobenius norm and
\|\cdot\|_{\rm op}
is the matrix operator norm induced by the Euclidian vector norm.
It follows from part (iii) of Lemma
S.3.1
applied to
A_{n}:=D_{n}
that

\displaystyle\frac{1}{n^{d}}\|D_{n}\|_{\rm F}^{2}\leq(2\pi)^{d}\int_{\theta\in[-\pi,\pi]^{d}}r_{n}^{2}(\theta)d\theta.

Thus, recalling
r_{n}(\theta)=g_{n}(\theta)+g_{n}(-\theta)
, Relation (
S.3.4
),
and using a change of variables, we obtain:

\displaystyle\frac{1}{n^{d}}\|D_{n}\|_{\rm F}^{2}\leq(2\pi)^{d}h_{n}^{2\beta+d}\left\{4\int_{\theta\in[-\pi,\pi]^{d}}g^{2}(\theta)d\theta\right\}=4\cdot(2\pi)^{d}\cdot\epsilon^{2}h_{n}^{2\beta+d}\|\varphi\|_{L^{2}}^{2d},

(S.3.12)

where we used (
S.3.3
). Applying (i) and (ii) of Lemma
S.3.1
, we obtain

\|B_{n,\xi}^{-1}\|_{\rm op}\leq\frac{1}{(2\pi)^{d}}\sup_{\theta\in[-\pi,\pi]^{d}}\left[f_{0n}(\theta)+\xi r_{n}(\theta)\right]^{-1}\leq\frac{2}{L},

(S.3.13)

since
r_{n}(\theta)\geq 0
and
f_{0n}(\theta)=L/(2\cdot(2\pi)^{d}),\ \theta\in[-\pi,\pi]
.
Combining (
S.3.11
) - (
S.3.13
),

\displaystyle{\rm KL}(\mathbb{P}_{1n},\mathbb{P}_{0n})

\displaystyle\leq\frac{1}{2}\|D_{n}\|_{F}^{2}\|B_{n,\xi}^{-1}\|_{\rm op}^{2}\leq\left(\frac{8\cdot(2\pi)^{d}\epsilon^{2}\|g\|_{L^{2}}^{2}}{L^{2}}\right)n^{d}h_{n}^{2\beta+d},

which is bounded, if we set

h_{n}=M\cdot n^{-d/(2\beta+d)}.

By picking
M=M_{\theta_{0}}
so that
[g(0)+g(0)\mathbbm{1}(\theta_{0}=0)]\cdot M^{\beta}=1
, we have that for all
sufficiently large
n
,

|f_{1n}(\theta_{0})-f_{0n}(\theta_{0})|=[g(0)+g(0)\mathbbm{1}(\theta_{0}=0)]\cdot M^{\beta}\cdot n^{-\frac{d\beta}{2\beta+d}}=n^{-\frac{d\beta}{2\beta+d}},

which is a lower bound in the estimation error. The proof is complete by appealing to Theorem 2.5(iii) of
Tsybakov 2008
.
∎

As argued in the proof of Theorem
6.1
, it suffices to focus on the case of scalar-valued processes

\{X(t),\ t\in\mathbb{R}^{d}\}
. As for the discrete-time case, we will introduce two models with spectral densities
f_{0,n}(\theta)
and
f_{1,n}(\theta)
,
and corresponding auto-covariances
C_{0,n}(t)
and
C_{1,n}(t)
. Consider the function:

f_{0,n}(\theta):=\epsilon\cdot\prod_{i=1}^{d}\phi(\delta_{n}\theta_{i}),\ \ \ \theta=(\theta_{i})_{i=1}^{d}\in\mathbb{R}^{d},

(S.3.14)

where
\phi(z)=e^{-z^{2}/2}/\sqrt{2\pi},\ z\in\mathbb{R}
is the standard Normal density.

With a straightforward change of variables, we obtain:

C_{0,n}(x)=\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}}e^{-\mathbbm{i}x^{\top}\theta}f_{0,n}(\theta)d\theta=\epsilon(2\pi)^{d/2}\cdot\delta_{n}^{-d}\prod_{i=1}^{d}\phi(x_{i}/\delta_{n}),

(S.3.15)

where we used the fact that
\int_{\mathbb{R}}e^{-\mathbbm{i}xu}\phi(u)du=\sqrt{2\pi}\phi(x)
.

As in the time-series setting, let

f_{1,n}(\theta)=f_{0,n}(\theta)+h_{n}^{\beta}\left[g\left(\frac{\theta-\theta_{0}}{h_{n}}\right)+g\left(\frac{\theta+\theta_{0}}{h_{n}}\right)\right],

(S.3.16)

where
g
is as in (
S.3.3
). Following
the proof of Theorem
6.1
, we will verify the following.

Properties:

(1)

f_{0,n},\ f_{1,n}\in{\cal P}_{C}(\beta,L)
, where the class
{\cal P}_{C}(\beta,L)
is defined in (
5.11
).

(2)

The functions
f_{0,n}
and
f_{1,n}
satisfy Relation (
S.3.5
).

(3)

The KL-divergence is bounded, i.e.,
\sup_{n}{\rm KL}(\mathbb{P}_{1n},\mathbb{P}_{0n})<\infty
, where
\mathbb{P}_{i,n}
are the probability
distributions of the data
\{X(\delta_{n}k),\ k\in\{1,\cdots,n\}^{d}\}
under the models
f_{i,n},\ i=0,1
.

Property (2) above is immediate by definition since the difference
f_{0,n}(\theta)-f_{1,n}(\theta)
is
constructed as in the proof of Theorem
6.1
.

Proof of Property (1):
The fact that
f_{0,n}\in{\cal P}_{C}(\beta,L)
is straightforward. Indeed, by (
S.3.15
), we have

\displaystyle\begin{split}&\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}}(1+\|x\|^{\beta})|C_{0,n}(x)|dx\leq_{c}\epsilon\cdot\delta_{n}^{-d}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}}(1+\|x\|^{\beta})e^{-\|x\|^{2}/2\delta_{n}^{2}}dx\\
&\quad=\epsilon\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}}(1+\|\delta_{n}\cdot u\|^{\beta})e^{-\|u\|^{2}/2}du\leq\epsilon\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}}(1+\|u\|^{\beta})e^{-\|u\|^{2}/2}du\leq L/2,\end{split}

(S.3.17)

for all
\delta_{n}\in(0,1)
and for a sufficiently small
\epsilon>0
. This follows from the fact that with
\delta_{n}\in(0,1)
, we have

\|\delta_{n}u\|^{\beta}\leq\|u\|^{\beta}
and the fact that
\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}}(1+\|u\|^{\beta})e^{-\|u\|^{2}/2}du<\infty
.
This ensures that (
5.11
) holds with
C
replaced by
C_{0,n}
and
L
by
L/2
.
That is,
f_{0,n}\in{\cal P}_{C}(\beta,L)
.

Now, we show that
f_{1,n}
defined in (
S.3.16
) belongs to
{\cal P}_{C}(\beta,L)
, by perhaps lowering the value of
\epsilon>0
.
Let

C_{g_{n}}(x):=\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}}e^{-\mathbbm{i}\theta^{\top}x}g_{n}(\theta)d\theta,

where
g_{n}
is as in (
S.3.4
).
As argued in the proof of Theorem
6.1
, in view of (
S.3.17
),
it suffices to show that

\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}}\left(1+\|x\|^{\beta}\right)|C_{g_{n}}(x)|dx\leq\frac{L}{4}.

(S.3.18)

Note that Relation (
S.3.8
) remains valid if
k\in\mathbb{Z}^{d}
therein is
replaced with
x\in\mathbb{R}^{d}
. Therefore, (
S.3.18
) follows by picking a possibly smaller value of
\epsilon>0
, provided

h_{n}^{\beta+d}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}}\left(1+\|x\|^{\beta}\right)\prod_{i=1}^{d}\left(c_{0}\wedge\frac{c_{\ell}}{|h_{n}x_{i}|^{\ell_{i}}}\right)dx<\infty,

for some
\ell\in\mathbb{N},\ i=1,\cdots,d
. Notice that for
h_{n}\in(0,1]
, we have

h_{n}^{\beta}(1+\|x\|^{\beta})\leq\left(1+\|h_{n}x\|^{\beta}\right)
, and hence the last integral is bounded
above by

\displaystyle h_{n}^{d}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}}\left(1+\|h_{n}x\|^{\beta}\right)\prod_{i=1}^{d}\left(c_{0}\wedge\frac{c_{\ell}}{|h_{n}x_{i}|^{\ell}}\right)dx=\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}}\left(1+\|u\|^{\beta}\right)\prod_{i=1}^{d}\left(c_{0}\wedge\frac{c_{\ell}}{|u_{i}|^{\ell}}\right)du,

where we used the change of variables
u:=h_{n}x
. Clearly, the last integral is finite
provided
\ell\geq\lfloor\beta\rfloor+2
. This implies that (
S.3.18
) holds with a suitably chosen

\epsilon>0
, showing that
f_{1,n}\in{\cal P}_{C}(\beta,L)
and completing the proof of Property (2).

Proof of Property (3):
Now, as in the proof of Theorem
6.1
we will bound the KL-divergence

{\rm KL}(\mathbb{P}_{1,n},\mathbb{P}_{0,n})
, where
\mathbb{P}_{i,n}
is the law of the Gaussian vector
\{X_{i}(\delta_{n}k),\ k\in\{1,\cdots,n\}^{d}\}
under the model

f_{i,n},\ i=0,1
.

Observe that the
\mathbb{Z}^{d}
-indexed stationary process
\{X_{i}(\delta_{n}k),\ k\in\mathbb{Z}^{d}\}
has the so-called folded
spectral density

\widetilde{f}_{i,h}(\theta):=\delta_{n}^{-d}\sum_{\ell\in\mathbb{Z}^{d}}f_{i,n}\left(\frac{\theta+2\pi\ell}{\delta_{n}}\right),\ \ \theta\in[-\pi,\pi]^{d},\ i=0,1.

(S.3.19)

We shall apply the same argument as in the proof of Theorem
6.1
based on
Samarov’s Lemmas
S.3.2
and
S.3.1
applied to the
folded spectral densities
.

For
\xi\in[0,1]
, let
D_{n}
and
B_{n,\xi}
be the covariance matrices of zero-mean Gaussian vectors
having spectral densities
\widetilde{r}_{n}(\theta):=\widetilde{f}_{1,n}(\theta)-\widetilde{f}_{0,n}(\theta)

and
\widetilde{f}_{0,n}(\theta)+\xi\widetilde{r}_{n}(\theta),\ \theta\in[-\pi,\pi]^{d}
, respectively, where

\displaystyle\widetilde{r}_{n}(\theta)=h_{n}^{\beta}\delta_{n}^{-d}\sum_{\ell\in\mathbb{Z}^{d}}\left[g\left(\frac{\theta+2\pi\ell}{h_{n}\delta_{n}}-\frac{\theta_{0}}{h_{n}}\right)+g\left(\frac{\theta+2\pi\ell}{h_{n}\delta_{n}}+\frac{\theta_{0}}{h_{n}}\right)\right].

Then, by Lemma
S.3.2
, we have

{\rm KL}(\mathbb{P}_{1,n},\mathbb{P}_{0,n})\leq\frac{1}{2}\|D_{n}\|_{\rm F}^{2}\|B_{n,\xi}^{-1}\|_{\rm op}^{2},

(S.3.20)

for some
\xi\in[0,1]
. As in (
S.3.12
) from Lemma
S.3.1
(iii) applied to
A_{n}:=D_{n}
, we obtain

\displaystyle\begin{split}\|D_{n}\|_{\rm F}^{2}&\leq(2\pi)^{d}\cdot n^{d}\int_{[-\pi,\pi]^{d}}\widetilde{r}_{n}(\theta)^{2}d\theta\\
&\leq 4\cdot(2\pi)^{d}\cdot n^{d}h_{n}^{2\beta}\delta_{n}^{-2d}\int_{[-\pi,\pi]^{d}}\sum_{\ell\in\mathbb{Z}^{d}}g\left(\frac{\theta+2\pi\ell}{h_{n}\delta_{n}}-\frac{\theta_{0}}{h_{n}}\right)^{2}d\theta\\
&=4\cdot(2\pi)^{d}\cdot n^{d}h_{n}^{2\beta}\delta_{n}^{-d}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}}g\left(\frac{u}{h_{n}}-\frac{\theta_{0}}{h_{n}}\right)^{2}du\\
&=4\cdot(2\pi)^{d}\cdot n^{d}h_{n}^{2\beta+d}\delta_{n}^{-d}\|g^{2}\|_{L^{2}}^{2}=4\epsilon\cdot(2\pi)^{d}\cdot n^{d}h_{n}^{2\beta+d}\delta_{n}^{-d}\|\varphi\|_{L^{2}}^{2d},\end{split}

(S.3.21)

where in the last two integrals we made changes of variables, and the last relation follows from the definition of
g
in (
S.3.3
).

Now, we deal with bounding
\|B_{n,\xi}^{-1}\|_{\rm op}
. Notice that
B_{n,\xi}
is the covariance matrix of
a Gaussian vector
\{X_{\xi}(\delta_{n}k),\ k\in\{1,\cdots,n\}^{d}\}
coming from a stationary process
Y(k)=X_{\xi}(\delta_{n}k),\ k\in\mathbb{Z}^{d}

with spectral density
\widetilde{f}_{0,n}(\theta)+\xi\widetilde{r}_{n}(\theta),\ \theta\in[-\pi,\pi]^{d}
, where
\widetilde{r}_{n}(\theta)\geq 0
and
\xi\in[0,1]
.
By Lemma
S.3.1
(ii), we then have that

\|B_{n,\xi}^{-1}\|_{\rm op}\|\leq\sup_{\theta\in[-\pi,\pi]^{d}}\left[\widetilde{f}_{0n}(\theta)+\xi\widetilde{r}_{n}(\theta)\right]^{-1}\leq\sup_{\theta\in[-\pi,\pi]^{d}}\left[\widetilde{f}_{0n}(\theta)\right]^{-1}.

Recalling the definition of
f_{0,n}
in (
S.3.14
) and the
folded
spectral density in (
S.3.19
),
we obtain

\widetilde{f}_{0,n}(\theta)\geq\epsilon\delta_{n}^{-d}\prod_{i=1}^{d}\phi(\theta_{i})\geq\epsilon\delta_{n}^{-d}e^{-d\pi^{2}/2}/(2\pi)^{d/2}

for
\theta\in[-\pi,\pi]^{d}
. Hence

\|B_{n,\xi}^{-1}\|_{\rm op}\leq\frac{1}{(2\pi)^{d}}\sup_{\theta\in[-\pi,\pi]^{d}}\left[\widetilde{f}_{0n}(\theta)\right]^{-1}\leq\frac{e^{d\pi^{2}/2}}{(2\pi)^{d/2}\cdot\epsilon}\cdot\delta_{n}^{d}

(S.3.22)

Finally, by (
S.3.20
), (
S.3.21
), and (
S.3.22
),

\displaystyle{\rm KL}(\mathbb{P}_{1n},\mathbb{P}_{0n})

\displaystyle\leq\frac{1}{2}\|D_{n}\|_{\rm F}^{2}\|B_{n,\xi}^{-1}\|_{\rm op}^{2}\leq c\cdot n^{d}h_{n}^{2\beta+d}\delta_{n}^{-d}\cdot\delta_{n}^{2d}=c\cdot(n\delta_{n})^{d}h_{n}^{2\beta+d},

where
c=2\epsilon^{-1}\|\varphi\|_{L^{2}}^{2d}e^{d\pi^{2}}
.
Thus, the KL-divergence is uniformly bounded if we set

h_{n}=M\cdot(n\delta_{n})^{-d/(2\beta+d)}.

Picking
M
so that
g(0)(1+\mathbbm{1}(\theta_{0}=0))\cdot M^{\beta}=1
, we have that

|f_{1n}(\theta_{0})-f_{0n}(\theta_{0})|=g(0)(1+\mathbbm{1}(\theta_{0}=0))\cdot M^{\beta}\cdot(n\delta_{n})^{-\frac{d\beta}{2\beta+d}}=(n\delta_{n})^{-\frac{d\beta}{2\beta+d}},

which, by appealing to Theorem 2.5(iii) of
Tsybakov 2008
,
yields the desired lower bound in estimation error in (
6.2
).
∎

The technical lemmas needed in the above proofs come from
Samarov 1977
. The first is a slight
extension to the
d
-dimensional case. We provide proofs below for the sake of completeness.

Lemma S.3.1
.

Let
a_{j},\ j\in\mathbb{Z}^{d}
be a sequence of numbers
such that
\sum_{j\in\mathbb{Z}^{d}}|a_{j}|^{2}<\infty
and
a_{j}=a_{-j}
. Let also
A_{n}
be a matrix of dimensions
n^{d}\times n^{d}
, whose

(j,k)
-th element equals
a_{j-k}
, where
j
and
k
are multi-indices that belong to
[0:n-1]^{d}:=\{0,1,\cdots,n-1\}^{d}
(i.e., the
(j,k)
-th element based on a natural ordering of the multi-indices of
[0:n-1]^{d}
). Finally, define

\alpha(\lambda)=(2\pi)^{-d}\sum_{j\in\mathbb{Z}^{d}}a_{j}e^{\mathbbm{i}j^{\top}\lambda}
, for
\lambda\in[-\pi,\pi]^{d}
.
Then, for the norms of
A_{n}
, the following claims are true.

(i)

\|A_{n}\|_{\rm op}\leq(2\pi)^{d}\cdot\sup_{\lambda\in[-\pi,\pi]^{d}}|\alpha(\lambda)|.

(ii)

If
A_{n}
is positive definite, then
\|A_{n}^{-1}\|_{\rm op}\leq(2\pi)^{-d}\cdot\sup_{\lambda\in[-\pi,\pi]^{d}}|1/\alpha(\lambda)|.

(iii)

n^{-d}\|A_{n}\|_{\rm F}^{2}\leq\sum_{j\in\mathbb{Z}^{d}}|a_{j}|^{2}=(2\pi)^{d}\int_{[-\pi,\pi]^{d}}\alpha^{2}(\lambda)d\lambda.

Let
N:=n^{d}
and use the notation
[0:n-1]^{d}:=\{0,1,\cdots,n-1\}^{d}.

We will follow the arguments in
Samarov 1977
.

(i)

Since
A_{n}
is a symmetric matrix, we have that

\|A_{n}\|_{\rm op}=\sup\left\{|x^{\top}A_{n}y|:\ \|x\|_{2}=\|y\|_{2}=1,\ x,y\in\mathbb{R}^{N}\right\},

where is the matrix (operator) norm induced by the Euclidian vector norm.
Now, let
x=(x_{i})_{i\in[0:n-1]^{d}}
and
y=(y_{i})_{i\in[0:n-1]^{d}}.
By Fourier inversion, we have that

a_{j-k}=\int_{[-\pi,\pi]^{d}}e^{-\mathbbm{i}(j-k)^{\top}\lambda}\alpha(\lambda)d\lambda.

Therefore,

\displaystyle|x^{\top}A_{n}y|
