\displaystyle\qquad\qquad\cdot\frac{|T-x|}{|T-(z+u)||T-u|}\sum_{y=0\vee(-x)}^{T\wedge(T-x)}\mathbbm{1}(x+y+z+u\in[0,T])\mathbbm{1}(u+y\in[0,T]).

Observing that

\displaystyle\frac{1}{T}\sum_{y=0\vee(-x)}^{T\wedge(T-x)}\mathbbm{1}(x+y+z+u\in[0,T])\mathbbm{1}(u+y\in[0,T])

\displaystyle\leq 1-\frac{|x|}{T}

\displaystyle\frac{1}{2\Delta}\sum_{u=-\Delta\vee(-\Delta-z)}^{\Delta\wedge(\Delta-z)}K\left(\frac{z+u}{\Delta}\right)K\left(\frac{u}{\Delta}\right)

\displaystyle\leq 1-\frac{|z|}{2\Delta}

\displaystyle\frac{|T-x|}{|T-(z+u)||T-u|}

\displaystyle\leq\frac{1}{|T|}

and the fact that
-T\leq x\leq T
and
-2\Delta\leq z\leq 2\Delta,
we see that the aforementioned terms are bounded by 1. Also, by Assumption
5
, we have that

\sum_{x=-T}^{T}\sum_{z=-2\Delta}^{2\Delta}C(x+z)C(z)dzdx<\infty.

Using the Dominated Convergence Theorem, we obtain that the quadruple summation, divided by
2\Delta/T
converges to a constant. Thus, it is asymptotically equivalent to
\Delta/T
as desired.

This, for
\lambda+1>\beta>0
, leads to the consistency rate

\mathscr{O}\left(\sqrt{\frac{\Delta_{n}}{|T_{n}|}}+\Delta_{n}^{-\beta}\cdot S(\Delta_{n})\right).

Considering
0<\beta\neq 1,
we see that the optimal consistency rate in this case essentially matches the one in Theorem
5.2
.

Observe that the regular variation only played a role in establishing asymptotic equivalence of the bias vanish rate. Indeed, for the rate of the variance, we only needed the integrability of the Covariance operator and the regular variation was not used. Also, recall that here the spectral density
f\in{\cal P}_{D}(\beta+\epsilon,L).
So the rate we should be comparing to is

|T_{n}|^{-\frac{\beta+\epsilon}{2(\beta+\epsilon)+1}}.

S.8.2
Examples on Assumptions
4.2
and
5

We present some examples of non-trivial processes that satisfy the Assumptions
4.2
and
5
, so as to demonstrate that
the assumptions are not vacuous. We first consider an example that satisfies Assumption
4.2
.

Example S.8.2
.

Consider the process

X(t)=Z(t)^{2}-1,

where
Z:=\{Z(t),t\in\mathbb{R}^{d}\}
is a zero-mean, real-valued stationary Gaussian process with standard normal marginals. Denote the stationary covariance of
Z
by
C_{Z}(\cdot)
which we assume to satisfy
\int_{u\in\mathbb{R}^{d}}\sup_{\lambda\in B(0,2\delta)}|C_{Z}(\lambda+u)|du<\infty
, for some small enough
\delta>0
.
This condition is quite mild and can be satisfied by covariances that are integrable and sufficiently smooth in the tail.
We verify that Assumption
4.2
holds for
X
. We start by considering
\widetilde{X}(t)=Z(t)^{2}
. It follows that

(a)

\mathbb{E}[\widetilde{X}(t)]=1

(b)

\mathbb{E}[\widetilde{X}(t_{1})\widetilde{X}(t_{2})]=1+2C_{Z}(t_{1}-t_{2})^{2}

(c)

\begin{aligned} \mathbb{E}[\widetilde{X}(t_{1})\widetilde{X}(t_{2})\widetilde{X}(t_{3})]&=15a_{2}^{2}a_{3}^{2}+3a_{2}^{2}b_{3}^{2}+3a_{2}^{2}c_{3}^{2}+3b_{2}^{2}a_{3}^{2}+3b_{2}^{2}b_{3}^{2}+b_{2}^{2}c_{3}^{2}+6a_{3}b_{3}a_{2}b_{2},\end{aligned}

(d)

\displaystyle\ \ \ \ \mathbb{E}[\widetilde{X}(t_{1})\widetilde{X}(t_{2})

\displaystyle\widetilde{X}(t_{3})\widetilde{X}(t_{4})]=105a_{2}^{2}a_{3}^{2}a_{4}^{2}+15a_{2}^{2}a_{3}^{2}b_{4}^{2}+15a_{2}^{2}a_{3}^{2}c_{4}^{2}+15a_{2}^{2}a_{3}^{2}d_{4}^{2}

\displaystyle+15a_{2}^{2}b_{3}^{2}a_{4}^{2}+9a_{2}^{2}b_{3}^{2}b_{4}^{2}+3a_{2}^{2}b_{3}^{2}c_{4}^{2}+3a_{2}^{2}b_{3}^{2}d_{4}^{2}

\displaystyle+15a_{2}^{2}c_{3}^{2}a_{4}^{2}+3a_{2}^{2}c_{3}^{2}b_{4}^{2}+9a_{2}^{2}c_{3}^{2}c_{4}^{2}+3a_{2}^{2}c_{3}^{2}d_{4}^{2}

\displaystyle+30a_{2}^{2}a_{3}b_{3}a_{4}b_{4}+30a_{2}^{2}a_{3}c_{3}a_{4}c_{4}+6a_{2}^{2}b_{3}c_{3}b_{4}c_{4}

\displaystyle+15b_{2}^{2}a_{3}^{2}a_{4}^{2}+9b_{2}^{2}a_{3}^{2}b_{4}^{2}+3b_{2}^{2}a_{3}^{2}c_{4}^{2}+3b_{2}^{2}a_{3}^{2}d_{4}^{2}

\displaystyle+9b_{2}^{2}b_{3}^{2}a_{4}^{2}+15b_{2}^{2}b_{3}^{2}b_{4}^{2}+3b_{2}^{2}b_{3}^{2}c_{4}^{2}+3b_{2}^{2}b_{3}^{2}d_{4}^{2}

\displaystyle+3b_{2}^{2}c_{3}^{2}a_{4}^{2}+3b_{2}^{2}c_{3}^{2}b_{4}^{2}+3b_{2}^{2}c_{3}^{2}c_{4}^{2}+b_{2}^{2}c_{3}^{2}d_{4}^{2}

\displaystyle+36b_{2}^{2}a_{3}b_{3}a_{4}b_{4}+12b_{2}^{2}a_{3}c_{3}a_{4}c_{4}+12b_{2}^{2}b_{3}c_{3}b_{4}c_{4}

\displaystyle+60a_{2}b_{2}a_{3}^{2}a_{4}b_{4}+36a_{2}b_{2}a_{4}b_{4}b_{3}^{2}+12a_{2}b_{2}c_{3}^{2}a_{4}b_{4}

\displaystyle+60a_{2}b_{2}a_{3}b_{3}a_{4}^{2}+36a_{2}b_{2}a_{3}b_{3}b_{4}^{2}+12a_{2}b_{2}a_{3}b_{3}c_{4}^{2}

\displaystyle+12a_{2}b_{2}a_{3}b_{3}d_{4}^{2}+24a_{2}b_{2}a_{3}b_{3}d_{4}^{2}+24a_{2}b_{2}a_{3}c_{3}b_{4}c_{4}+24a_{2}b_{2}b_{3}c_{3}a_{4}c_{4},

where

\displaystyle a_{2}

\displaystyle=C_{Z}(t_{1}-t_{2})

\displaystyle a_{3}

\displaystyle=C_{Z}(t_{1}-t_{3})

\displaystyle b_{2}

\displaystyle=\sqrt{1-C_{Z}(t_{1}-t_{2})^{2}}

\displaystyle b_{3}

\displaystyle=\frac{C_{Z}(t_{2}-t_{3})-C_{Z}(t_{1}-t_{2})\cdot C_{Z}(t_{1}-t_{3})}{\sqrt{1-C_{Z}(t_{1}-t_{2})^{2}}}

\displaystyle c_{3}

\displaystyle=\sqrt{1-C_{Z}(t_{1}-t_{3})^{2}-\frac{\left[C_{Z}(t_{2}-t_{3})-C_{Z}(t_{1}-t_{2})\cdot C_{Z}(t_{1}-t_{3})\right]^{2}}{1-C_{Z}(t_{1}-t_{2})^{2}}}

\displaystyle a_{4}

\displaystyle=C_{Z}(t_{1}-t_{4})

\displaystyle b_{4}

\displaystyle=\frac{C_{Z}(t_{2}-t_{4})-C_{Z}(t_{1}-t_{2})C_{Z}(t_{1}-t_{4})}{\sqrt{1-C_{Z}(t_{1}-t_{2})^{2}}}

\displaystyle c_{4}

\displaystyle=\frac{C_{Z}(t_{3}-t_{4})-C_{Z}(t_{1}-t_{3})C_{Z}(t_{1}-t_{4})-b_{3}b_{4}}{c_{3}}

\displaystyle d_{4}

\displaystyle=\sqrt{1-a_{4}^{2}-b_{4}^{2}-c_{4}^{2}}.

After centering the process as
X(t)=\widetilde{X}(t)-1
and simplifying the aforementioned expressions, we end up with the following moments:

(a)

\mathbb{E}[X(t)]=0

(b)

\mathbb{E}[X(t_{1})X(t_{2})]=2C_{Z}(t_{1}-t_{2})^{2}

(c)

\mathbb{E}[X(t_{1})X(t_{2})X(t_{3})]=8C_{Z}(t_{1}-t_{2})C_{Z}(t_{1}-t_{3})C_{Z}(t_{2}-t_{3})
,

(d)

\displaystyle\quad\ \mathbb{E}[X(t_{1})X(t_{2})

\displaystyle X(t_{3})X(t_{4})]=4C_{Z}(t_{1}-t_{2})^{2}C_{Z}(t_{3}-t_{4})^{2}

\displaystyle+4C_{Z}(t_{1}-t_{3})^{2}C_{Z}(t_{2}-t_{4})^{2}+4C_{Z}(t_{1}-t_{4})^{2}C_{Z}(t_{2}-t_{3})^{2}

\displaystyle+16C_{Z}(t_{1}-t_{3})C_{Z}(t_{1}-t_{4})C_{Z}(t_{2}-t_{3})C_{Z}(t_{2}-t_{4})

\displaystyle+16C_{Z}(t_{1}-t_{2})C_{Z}(t_{1}-t_{4})C_{Z}(t_{2}-t_{3})C_{Z}(t_{3}-t_{4})

\displaystyle+16C_{Z}(t_{1}-t_{2})C_{Z}(t_{1}-t_{3})C_{Z}(t_{2}-t_{4})C_{Z}(t_{3}-t_{4})

.

Using (d) above, we obtain that

\mathbb{E}|X(t)|^{4}=60C_{Z}(0)^{4}=60<\infty,

showing that (a) of Condition
4.2
holds.

By the definition of the cumulants in Definition
S.5.1
we obtain that

\displaystyle{\rm cum}(X(t_{1}),X(t_{2}),X(t_{3}),X(t_{4}))

\displaystyle=16C_{Z}(t_{1}-t_{3})C_{Z}(t_{1}-t_{4})C_{Z}(t_{2}-t_{3})C_{Z}(t_{2}-t_{4})

\displaystyle+16C_{Z}(t_{1}-t_{2})C_{Z}(t_{1}-t_{4})C_{Z}(t_{2}-t_{3})C_{Z}(t_{3}-t_{4})

\displaystyle+16C_{Z}(t_{1}-t_{2})C_{Z}(t_{1}-t_{3})C_{Z}(t_{2}-t_{4})C_{Z}(t_{3}-t_{4}).

Then, Assumption
4.2
(b) is also satisfied, since

\displaystyle\sup_{w\in\mathbb{R}^{d}}

\displaystyle\int_{u\in\mathbb{R}^{d}}\int_{v\in\mathbb{R}^{d}}\sup_{\lambda_{1},\lambda_{2},\lambda_{3}\in B(0,\delta)}|{\rm cum}(X(\lambda_{1}+u),X(\lambda_{2}+v),X(\lambda_{3}+w),X(0))|dvdu

\displaystyle\leq 16\sup_{w\in\mathbb{R}^{d}}\int_{u\in\mathbb{R}^{d}}\int_{v\in\mathbb{R}^{d}}\sup_{\lambda_{1},\lambda_{2},\lambda_{3}\in B(0,\delta)}

\displaystyle\quad\Big\{|C_{Z}(\lambda_{1}-\lambda_{3}+u-w)C_{Z}(\lambda_{1}+u)C_{Z}(\lambda_{2}-\lambda_{3}+v-w)C_{Z}(\lambda_{2}+v)|

\displaystyle\quad+|C_{Z}(\lambda_{1}-\lambda_{2}+u-v)C_{Z}(\lambda_{1}+u)C_{Z}(\lambda_{2}-\lambda_{3}+v-w)C_{Z}(\lambda_{3}+w)|

\displaystyle\quad+|C_{Z}(\lambda_{1}-\lambda_{2}+u-v)C_{Z}(\lambda_{1}-\lambda_{3}+u-w)C_{Z}(\lambda_{2}+v)C_{Z}(\lambda_{3}+w)|\Big\}dvdu

\displaystyle\leq 16|C_{Z}(0)|^{2}\int_{u\in\mathbb{R}^{d}}\int_{v\in\mathbb{R}^{d}}\sup_{\lambda_{1},\lambda_{2}\in B(0,\delta)}\Big\{|C_{Z}(\lambda_{1}+u)C_{Z}(\lambda_{2}+v)|

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad\quad+|C_{Z}(\lambda_{1}-\lambda_{2}+u-v)C_{Z}(\lambda_{1}+u)|

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad\quad+|C_{Z}(\lambda_{1}-\lambda_{2}+u-v)C_{Z}(\lambda_{2}+v)|\Big\}dvdu

\displaystyle\leq 48|C_{Z}(0)|^{2}\left(\int_{u\in\mathbb{R}^{d}}\sup_{\lambda\in B(0,2\delta)}|C_{Z}(\lambda+u)|du\right)^{2}<\infty.

∎

Next, we present an example inspired by the linear processes in Proposition 4.1 of
Panaretos and
Tavakoli 2013
. Assume that
\mathbb{H}
is a separable (typically infinite-dimensional) Hilbert space. Let
\epsilon_{t},\ t\in\mathbb{Z}
be iid random elements of
\mathbb{H}
such that
\mathbb{E}\|\epsilon_{0}\|^{4}<\infty
and consider a sequence of bounded linear operators
A_{s}:\mathbb{H}\to\mathbb{H},\ s\in\mathbb{Z}
. Define

X(t)=\sum_{s\in\mathbb{Z}}A_{s}\epsilon_{t-s},\ t\in\mathbb{Z}.

(S.8.1)

In the following lemma, we show that the real process
X(t)
is well defined under a mild square-summability condition on the operator norms of the coefficients. To this end,
let
{\cal L}^{2}(\mathbb{H})
denote the Hilbert space of
\mathbb{H}
-valued random elements equipped with the inner product
\langle A,B\rangle_{\mathscr{L}^{2}}=\mathbb{E}\langle A,B\rangle

for all
\mathbb{H}
-valued random elements
A
and
B
such that
\mathbb{E}[\|A\|^{2}+\|B\|^{2}]<\infty
. The resulting norm in
{\cal L}^{2}(\mathbb{H})
will be denoted by
\|\cdot\|_{{\cal L}^{2}(\mathbb{H})}
.

Lemma S.8.1
.

Assume that the operator norms of
\{A_{s},\ s\in\mathbb{Z}\}
are square summable, namely that

\sum_{s\in\mathbb{Z}}\|A_{s}\|_{\rm op}^{2}<\infty.

(S.8.2)

Then, the series in (
S.8.1
) converges in
\|\cdot\|_{{\cal L}^{2}(\mathbb{H})}
and the process
\{X(t),\ t\in\mathbb{Z}\}
defined.

Let
\Sigma_{\epsilon}=\mathbb{E}[\epsilon_{0}\otimes\epsilon_{0}]
be the covariance operator of every
\epsilon_{t},\ t\in\mathbb{Z}
.
We start by defining

X^{(N)}(t)=\sum_{|s|\leq N}A_{s}\epsilon_{t-s}\qquad{\rm and}\qquad X^{-(N)}(t)=\sum_{|s|>N}A_{s}\epsilon_{t-s}.

(S.8.3)

We have that

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\langle X^{(N)}(t),X^{(N)}(t)\rangle

\displaystyle=\sum_{|s_{1}|\leq N}\sum_{|s_{2}|\leq N}\mathbb{E}\langle A_{s_{1}}\epsilon_{t-s_{1}},A_{s_{2}}\epsilon_{t-s_{2}}\rangle

\displaystyle=\sum_{|s|\leq N}\mathbb{E}\langle A_{s}\epsilon_{t-s},A_{s}\epsilon_{t-s}\rangle=\sum_{|s|\leq N}\mathbb{E}\langle\epsilon_{t-s},A_{s}^{\star}A_{s}\epsilon_{t-s}\rangle,

where the second equality follows by the independence of the
\epsilon
’s.
Let now
\{e_{j}\}
be a CONS of
\mathbb{H}
that diagonalizes
\Sigma_{\epsilon}
. Then, we can express the
\epsilon
’s as

\epsilon_{t-s}=\sum_{j=1}^{\infty}Z_{t-s,j}e_{j},

where
Z_{s,j}:=\langle\epsilon_{s},e_{j}\rangle,
are independent in
s
because the
\epsilon_{s}
’s are iid. Also, because of the choice
of
\{e_{j}\}
as the eigenvectors of the covariance operator
\Sigma_{\epsilon}
, we have that for each fixed
s
, the
Z_{s,j}
’s are uncorrelated in
j
:

\mathbb{E}\left[Z_{s,i}\overline{Z_{s,j}}\right]=\lambda_{i}\cdot\delta_{i-j}.

Using those, we obtain

\displaystyle\begin{split}\mathbb{E}\left\langle X^{(N)}(t),X^{(N)}(t)\right\rangle&=\sum_{|s|\leq N}\mathbb{E}\left\langle\sum_{k}e_{k}Z_{t-s,k},A_{s}^{\star}A_{s}\sum_{\ell}e_{\ell}Z_{t-s,{\ell}}\right\rangle\\
&=\sum_{|s|\leq N}\sum_{k}\sum_{\ell}\mathbb{E}\left[Z_{t-s,k}\overline{Z_{t-s,{\ell}}}\right]\cdot\langle e_{k},A_{s}^{\star}A_{s}e_{\ell}\rangle\\
&=\sum_{|s|\leq N}\sum_{k}\lambda_{k}\cdot\langle e_{k},A_{s}^{\star}A_{s}e_{k}\rangle\leq\sum_{k}\lambda_{k}\sum_{|s|\leq N}\langle e_{k},A_{s}^{\star}A_{s}e_{k}\rangle\\
&\leq{\rm tr}(\Sigma_{\epsilon})\cdot\sum_{|s|\leq N}\|A_{s}^{\star}A_{s}\|_{\rm op}\leq{\rm tr}(\Sigma_{\epsilon})\cdot\sum_{|s|\leq N}\|A_{s}\|_{\rm op}^{2}<\infty.\end{split}

(S.8.4)

With a similar argument to (
S.8.4
), one has that for
M<N

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\|X^{(N)}(t)-X^{(M)}(t)\|^{2}\leq{\rm tr}(\Sigma_{\epsilon})\sum_{M<|s|\leq N}\|A_{s}\|_{\rm op}^{2}\to 0,

as
N,M\to\infty
. This shows that the sequence
\{X^{(N)}(t)\}_{N\in\mathbb{N}}
is a Cauchy sequence in the Hilbert space

\left(\mathscr{L}^{2}(\mathbb{H}),\langle\cdot,\cdot\rangle_{\mathscr{L}^{2}}\right)
, where
\langle A,B\rangle_{\mathscr{L}^{2}}=\mathbb{E}\langle A,B\rangle
for
A,B
random elements of
\mathbb{H}
.
Thus, the limit of this sequence exists and

X^{(N)}(t)\to X(t)\in\mathscr{L}^{2}(\mathbb{H}),

which completes the proof.
∎

Proposition S.8.2
.

Let
X(t)
defined as in (
S.8.1
). Assume that
\{A_{s},\ s\in\mathbb{Z}\}
are Hilbert-Schmidt operators with
\sum_{s\in\mathbb{Z}}\|A_{s}\|_{\rm HS}<\infty
. Moreover, letting

Z_{s,j}=\langle\epsilon_{s},e_{j}\rangle
, where
\{e_{j}\}
is a CONS diagonalizing
\Sigma_{\epsilon}:=\mathbb{E}[\epsilon_{0}\otimes\epsilon_{0}]
, assume that

\sum_{\ell_{1},\ell_{2},\ell_{3},\ell_{4}}{\rm cum}(Z_{0,\ell_{1}},Z_{0,\ell_{2}},Z_{0,\ell_{3}},Z_{0,\ell_{4}})^{2}\leq B<\infty.

Then, the process
\{X(t),\ t\in\mathbb{Z}\}
satisfies Assumption
5
.

Recall that part
(a)
of Assumption
5
entails the finite fourth moment of
\|X(t)\|
.

Let
X^{(N)}(t)
and
X^{-(N)}(t)
be as defined in (
S.8.3
).
Then, for every
k\in\mathbb{N}
such that
\mathbb{E}\|\epsilon_{t}\|^{k}<\infty
, we have that

\displaystyle\begin{split}\mathbb{E}\left\|X^{-(N)}(t)\right\|^{k}&\leq\sum_{|s_{1}|,\ldots,|s_{k}|>N}\|A_{s_{1}}\|_{\rm op}\ldots\|A_{s_{k}}\|_{\rm op}\mathbb{E}\left(\|\epsilon_{t-s_{1}}\|\ldots\|\epsilon_{t-s_{k}}\|\right)\\
&\leq\sum_{|s_{1}|,\ldots,|s_{k}|>N}\|A_{s_{1}}\|_{\rm op}\ldots\|A_{s_{k}}\|_{\rm op}\mathbb{E}\left(\|\epsilon_{t-s_{1}}\|^{k}\right)^{1/k}\ldots\mathbb{E}\left(\|\epsilon_{t-s_{k}}\|^{k}\right)^{1/k}\\
&=\mathbb{E}\|\epsilon_{0}\|^{k}\cdot\sum_{|s_{1}|,\ldots,|s_{k}|>N}\|A_{s_{1}}\|_{\rm op}\ldots\|A_{s_{k}}\|_{\rm op}\\
&=\mathbb{E}\|\epsilon_{0}\|^{k}\cdot\left(\sum_{|s|>N}\|A_{s}\|_{\rm op}\right)^{k}\to 0,\ \textrm{as}\ N\to\infty,\end{split}

(S.8.5)

where the inequality in the second line follows from the generalized Hölder inequality
(
Hardy et al. 1952
, cf. Theorem 11 of)
and we used that
\|A_{s}\|_{\rm op}\leq\|A_{s}\|_{\rm HS}
.
Hence, we have
{\cal L}^{k}(\mathbb{H})
-convergence of
X_{t}^{(N)}
to
X_{t},
in the sense that

\lim_{N\to\infty}\left(\mathbb{E}\left\|X(t)-X^{(N)}(t)\right\|_{\mathbb{H}}^{k}\right)^{1/k}=0.

These previous calculations also show directly that
\mathbb{E}\|X_{t}\|^{k}<\infty.
Specifically, for
k=4
, part
(a)
is proved.

Now, for part
(b)
, as in the proof of Lemma
S.8.1
, letting
\{e_{j}\}
be a CONS diagonalizing
\Sigma_{\epsilon}=\mathbb{E}[\epsilon_{0}\otimes\epsilon_{0}]
,
we write

\displaystyle A_{s}=\sum_{i,j}a_{ij}(s)e_{i}\otimes e_{j}\ \ \mbox{ and }\ \ \epsilon_{t-s}=\sum_{k}Z_{t-s,k}e_{k},

with
Z_{s,k}:=\langle\epsilon_{s},e_{k}\rangle
. Note that
\{e_{i}\otimes e_{j}\}
is a CONS in the Hilbert space
\mathbb{X}
of Hilbert-Schmidt operators on
\mathbb{H}
equipped with
\langle\cdot,\cdot\rangle_{\rm HS}

and the above expression for
A_{s}
converges in
\|\cdot\|_{\rm HS}
. Let also

\displaystyle A_{s,i\cdot}

\displaystyle:=\sum_{j}a_{ij}(s)e_{i}\otimes e_{j}

\displaystyle X_{i}(t)

\displaystyle:=\langle X(t),e_{i}\rangle=\sum_{s\in\mathbb{Z}}\sum_{j}a_{ij}(s)Z_{t-s,j}=\sum_{s\in\mathbb{Z}}A_{s,i\cdot}\epsilon_{t-s},

so that
X(t)=\sum_{i}X_{i}(t)e_{i}
. Recall the representation in Proposition
S.5.2
(see also
(
4.9
) in the main paper). For notational simplicity suppose that the process
X(t)
is real relative to the CONS
\{e_{i}\}
, i.e., all the
X_{i}(t)
’s are
real random variables.

We start by exploiting the multilinearity of the cumulants and the fact that
\epsilon_{t}
’s are iid. We have by Proposition
S.5.2
that

{\rm cum}\left(X(u),X(v),X(w),X(0)\right)
equals:

\displaystyle\left|\sum_{i}\sum_{j}{\rm cum}\left(X_{i}(u),X_{j}(v),X_{i}(w),X_{j}(0)\right)\right|

\displaystyle=\left|\sum_{i}\sum_{j}{\rm cum}\left(\sum_{s_{1}\in\mathbb{Z}}A_{s_{1},i\cdot}\epsilon_{u-s_{1}},\sum_{s_{2}\in\mathbb{Z}}A_{s_{2},j\cdot}\epsilon_{v-s_{2}},\sum_{s_{3}\in\mathbb{Z}}A_{s_{3},i\cdot}\epsilon_{w-s_{3}},\sum_{s_{4}\in\mathbb{Z}}A_{s_{4},j\cdot}\epsilon_{-s_{4}}\right)\right|

\displaystyle=\left|\sum_{i}\sum_{j}{\rm cum}\left(\sum_{s_{1}\in\mathbb{Z}}A_{u-s_{1},i\cdot}\epsilon_{s_{1}},\sum_{s_{2}\in\mathbb{Z}}A_{v-s_{2},j\cdot}\epsilon_{s_{2}},\sum_{s_{3}\in\mathbb{Z}}A_{w-s_{3},i\cdot}\epsilon_{s_{3}},\sum_{s_{4}\in\mathbb{Z}}A_{-s_{4},j\cdot}\epsilon_{s_{4}}\right)\right|

\displaystyle=\left|\sum_{i}\sum_{j}\sum_{s_{1}\in\mathbb{Z}}\sum_{s_{2}\in\mathbb{Z}}\sum_{s_{3}\in\mathbb{Z}}\sum_{s_{4}\in\mathbb{Z}}{\rm cum}\left(A_{u-s_{1},i\cdot}\epsilon_{s_{1}},A_{v-s_{2},j\cdot}\epsilon_{s_{2}},A_{w-s_{3},i\cdot}\epsilon_{s_{3}},A_{-s_{4},j\cdot}\epsilon_{s_{4}}\right)\right|

(S.8.6)

\displaystyle=\left|\sum_{i}\sum_{j}\sum_{s\in\mathbb{Z}}{\rm cum}\left(A_{u-s,i\cdot}\epsilon_{s},A_{v-s,j\cdot}\epsilon_{s},A_{w-s,i\cdot}\epsilon_{s},A_{-s,j\cdot}\epsilon_{s}\right)\right|,

(S.8.7)

where (
S.8.7
) follows from the fact that
\epsilon_{t}
’s are iid and (
S.8.6
) will be justified in the end of this proof.

Continuing, (
S.8.7
) is equal to

\displaystyle\begin{split}&\left|\sum_{i}\sum_{j}\sum_{s\in\mathbb{Z}}\sum_{\ell_{1},\ell_{2},\ell_{3},\ell_{4}}a_{i\ell_{1}}(u-s)a_{j\ell_{2}}(v-s)a_{i\ell_{3}}(w-s)a_{j\ell_{4}}(-s){\rm cum}\left(Z_{s,\ell_{1}},Z_{s,\ell_{2}},Z_{s,\ell_{3}},Z_{s,\ell_{4}}\right)\right|\\
&=\left|\sum_{i}\sum_{j}\sum_{s\in\mathbb{Z}}\sum_{\ell_{1},\ell_{2},\ell_{3},\ell_{4}}a_{i\ell_{1}}(u-s)a_{j\ell_{2}}(v-s)a_{i\ell_{3}}(w-s)a_{j\ell_{4}}(-s){\rm cum}\left(Z_{0,\ell_{1}},Z_{0,\ell_{2}},Z_{0,\ell_{3}},Z_{0,\ell_{4}}\right)\right|.\end{split}

(S.8.8)

Changing the order of summation and applying the Cauchy-Schwarz inequality over
\sum_{\ell_{1},\cdots,\ell_{4}}
, we have that (
S.8.8
) is bounded above by

\displaystyle\Big|\sum_{s}\sqrt{\sum_{\ell_{1},\cdots,\ell_{4}}{\rm cum}(Z_{0,\ell_{1}},Z_{0,\ell_{2}},Z_{0,\ell_{3}},Z_{0,\ell_{4}})^{2}}

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad\cdot\sqrt{\sum_{\ell_{1},\cdots,\ell_{4}}\Big(\sum_{i,j}\Big[a_{i\ell_{1}}(u-s)a_{j\ell_{2}}(v-s)a_{i\ell_{3}}(w-s)a_{j\ell_{4}}(-s)\Big]\Big)^{2}}

\displaystyle\leq B\sum_{s\in\mathbb{Z}}\left(\sum_{\ell_{1},\cdots,\ell_{4}}\Big(\sum_{i}a_{i\ell_{1}}(u-s)^{2}\Big)\Big(\sum_{i}a_{i\ell_{3}}(w-s)^{2}\Big)\Big(\sum_{j}a_{j\ell_{2}}(v-s)^{2}\Big)\Big(\sum_{j}a_{j\ell_{4}}(-s)^{2}\Big)\right)^{1/2},

\displaystyle=B\sum_{s\in\mathbb{Z}}\|A_{u-s}\|_{\rm HS}\|A_{v-s}\|_{\rm HS}\|A_{w-s}\|_{\rm HS}\|A_{-s}\|_{\rm HS},

where the above inequality follows by applying the Cauchy-Schwarz inequality twice – once over
\sum_{i}
and once over
\sum_{j}
.
The last relation follows from the fact that
\|A_{t}\|_{\rm HS}^{2}=\sum_{\ell,i}a_{i\ell}(t)^{2}
.

Thus, we finally obtain:

\displaystyle\sup_{w\in\mathbb{Z}}\sum_{u\in\mathbb{Z}}\sum_{v\in\mathbb{Z}}\left|\sum_{i}\sum_{j}{\rm cum}\left(X_{i}(u),X_{j}(v),X_{i}(w),X_{j}(0)\right)\right|

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad\leq\sup_{w\in\mathbb{Z}}\sum_{u\in\mathbb{Z}}\sum_{v\in\mathbb{Z}}B\sum_{s\in\mathbb{Z}}\|A_{u-s}\|_{\rm HS}\|A_{v-s}\|_{\rm HS}\|A_{w-s}\|_{\rm HS}\|A_{-s}\|_{\rm HS}

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad\leq B\sup_{w\in\mathbb{Z}}\|A_{w}\|_{\rm HS}\cdot\left(\sum_{s\in\mathbb{Z}}\|A_{u-s}\|_{\rm HS}\right)^{3}<\infty.

Now, it only remains to justify the equality (
S.8.6
). We will use
X^{(N)}(t)
and
X^{-(N)}(t)
again. The calculations in (
S.8.5
) imply again by the generalized Hölder inequality and the Dominated Convergence Theorem that

\mathbb{E}\left[\lim_{N\to\infty}X_{i}^{(N)}(u)X_{j}^{(N)}(v)X_{i}^{(N)}(w)X_{j}^{(N)}(0)\right]=\lim_{N\to\infty}\mathbb{E}\left[X_{i}^{(N)}(u)X_{j}^{(N)}(v)X_{i}^{(N)}(w)X_{j}^{(N)}(0)\right].

To this end, we introduce some notation. For each pair
m=(m_{1},m_{2})\in\{(u,i),(v,j),(w,i),(0,j)\}
, we write
X_{m}^{N}
for
X_{m_{2}}^{N}(m_{1})
.
For example, for
m=(u,i)
we have that
X_{m}^{(N)}=X_{i}^{(N)}(u).

Thus, using the definition of cumulants, we obtain

\displaystyle{\rm cum}\left(X_{i}(u),X_{j}(v),X_{i}(w),X_{j}(0)\right)

\displaystyle=\sum_{\nu=(\nu_{1},\ldots,\nu_{q})}(-1)^{q-1}(q-1)!\prod_{l=1}^{q}\mathbb{E}\left[\prod_{m\in\nu_{l}}\lim_{N\to\infty}X_{m}^{(N)}\right]

\displaystyle=\sum_{\nu=(\nu_{1},\ldots,\nu_{q})}(-1)^{q-1}(q-1)!\prod_{l=1}^{q}\mathbb{E}\left[\lim_{N\to\infty}\prod_{m\in\nu_{l}}X_{m}^{(N)}\right]

\displaystyle=\lim_{N\to\infty}\sum_{\nu=(\nu_{1},\ldots,\nu_{q})}(-1)^{q-1}(q-1)!\prod_{l=1}^{q}\mathbb{E}\left[\prod_{m\in\nu_{l}}X_{m}^{(N)}\right]

\displaystyle=\lim_{N\to\infty}{\rm cum}\Bigg(\sum_{|s_{1}|\leq|N|}A_{s_{1},i\cdot}\epsilon_{u-s_{1}},\sum_{|s_{2}|\leq|N|}A_{s_{2},j\cdot}\epsilon_{v-s_{2}},

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad\sum_{|s_{3}|\leq|N|}A_{s_{3},i\cdot}\epsilon_{w-s_{3}},\sum_{|s_{4}|\leq|N|}A_{s_{4},i\cdot}\epsilon_{-s_{4}}\Bigg)

\displaystyle=\lim_{N\to\infty}\sum_{|s_{1}|,|s_{2}|,|s_{3}|,|s_{4}|\leq|N|}{\rm cum}\left(A_{s_{1},i\cdot}\epsilon_{u-s_{1}},A_{s_{2},j\cdot}\epsilon_{v-s_{2}},A_{s_{3},i\cdot}\epsilon_{w-s_{3}},A_{s_{4},i\cdot}\epsilon_{-s_{4}}\right)

\displaystyle=\sum_{|s_{1}|,|s_{2}|,|s_{3}|,|s_{4}|\in\mathbb{Z}}{\rm cum}\left(A_{s_{1},i\cdot}\epsilon_{u-s_{1}},A_{s_{2},j\cdot}\epsilon_{v-s_{2}},A_{s_{3},i\cdot}\epsilon_{w-s_{3}},A_{s_{4},i\cdot}\epsilon_{-s_{4}}\right),

where the sum is over all unordered partitions of
\{(u,i),(v,j),(w,i),(0,j)\}
. The proof is complete.

∎
